\section{Phase 5 SoC --- ASAP7 P\&R Run History (M11)}\label{cr:run:phase5}

\begin{record}

Full-SoC ASAP7 hierarchical place-and-route campaign (M11). Mirrors
\cref{cr:run:asap7} (CPU/\allowbreak{}GPU). The SoC
integrates the CPU + GPU as hard macros with the crossbar / AXI-Lite
interconnect / DMA / peripherals / PLL stub synthesized flat. Branch
\code{feature/phase7-mixed-signal-pll}.

\begin{quote}
\warn{} \textbf{Every "SoC power" figure in this document is
fabric-only.} \code{report_power} attributes \textbf{0.00 W to both hard
macros} (CPU, GPU) in every run below --- the bulk of the design
contributes nothing to the reported total. Every mW value quoted here,
including the run-14 M11 sign-off figure, is fabric + glue power, not
true SoC power. Root cause, re-measurement, and how to bound the gap:
see \textbf{§ Power figure caveat (bead
\bead{ew3}) (\cref{cr:run:phase5:power-figure-caveat-macro-power-omitted-from-every-figure-bead-ew3})}.
\end{quote}

\begin{quote}
\warn{} \textbf{Every "Routing DRC 0" / "antenna 0" claim in this
document is unsupported --- detailed routing never committed a wire.}
Measured directly on run 14\textquotesingle s own artifacts:
\code{final/def/soc_top.def} has zero \texttt{ROUTED\ } net records and
\texttt{route\_\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}wirelength\_\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}max\ =\allowbreak{}\ 0}.
\code{OpenROAD.DetailedRouting} (step 38) errored during pin access
(\texttt{[ERROR\ DRT-\allowbreak{}0073]\ No\ access\ point\ for\ u\_\allowbreak{}cpu/\allowbreak{}apb\_\allowbreak{}paddr\_\allowbreak{}i[5]}
/ \code{u_gpu/m_axi_araddr[19]}) before track assignment ever ran; a
local, non-upstream patch to LibreLane\textquotesingle s \code{drt.tcl}
wraps the whole call in \texttt{catch\ \{\}} and lets the flow continue
anyway, so every downstream checker reports 0 violations because nothing
was routed to violate. This is \textbf{not} a run-14-specific or
SoC-specific defect: it reproduces on both GPU 571 MHz sign-off runs and
every CPU tuning run in the \#96 campaign --- see \textbf{§ Routing /
physical-closure caveat (bead
\bead{xy6}) (\cref{cr:run:phase5:routing-physical-closure-caveat-detailed-routing-committed-zero-wires-bead-xy6})}
for the full table and what remains valid.
\end{quote}

\begin{quote}
\warn{} \textbf{Every setup/\allowbreak{}hold WNS/TNS number in this
document from before 2026-09-16 is \code{OpenROAD.STAMidPNR-3} output,
produced WITHOUT an in-session \code{global_route}} (bead
\code{claude_verilog_test-8f3}, found/\allowbreak{}fixed 2026-09-16).
\code{sta/corner.tcl} calls
\texttt{estimate\_\allowbreak{}parasitics\ -\allowbreak{}global\_\allowbreak{}routing}
on a loaded ODB, and OpenROAD does not recover GRT parasitics from a
saved ODB alone --- so these numbers run on largely zero-wire /
unannotated parasitics. Proven on an isolated CPU-block run of this same
design family: 94.44\% of drivers unannotated, falsely reporting 0/0/0
setup and hold violations. Every run in this document (14, 23, and the
whole \#96 campaign) predates the fix
(\code{STA_POSTGRT_INSESSION_GRT=1}, now applied to the shared LibreLane
tree) and has \textbf{not} been re-measured with it --- treat as
\textbf{unvalidated, not confirmed-wrong}. The one exception: the
honest, in-session-GRT current-RTL figure from run \code{w3a-sta-7}
documented in \textbf{§ Power figure caveat, "UPDATE 2026-09-16 (bead
\bead{86a}
closing)" (\cref{cr:run:phase5:power-figure-caveat-macro-power-omitted-from-every-figure-bead-ew3})}
--- read that block for the replacement timing numbers (setup WNS
−2084.08 ps / TNS −2.19×10⁷ ps / 41,864 violators; hold WNS −917.23 ps /
718 violators), which is the current-RTL basis, not a re-run of run
14/23 themselves (their run directories and RTL vintage are gone --- see
the "Bead \code{0p6} note" in that same section). Full re-validation
inventory, run-by-run classification, and what is/isn\textquotesingle t
recoverable: bead \code{claude_verilog_test-0p6}.
\end{quote}

\begin{quote}
\ok{} \textbf{UPDATE 2026-09-18 (bead \code{claude_verilog_test-0d0})
--- the SoC HAS since been re-measured on the fixed flow; the
\code{w3a-sta-7} replacement figures above are themselves superseded.}
Three full ASAP7 SoC flows have now run end-to-end with
\code{STA_POSTGRT_INSESSION_GRT=1}, in-session GRT parasitics and a
proven annotation gate. The current honest current-RTL basis is the
latest of them, \code{pnr/asap7/soc/runs/RUN_2026-09-17_20-41-11}
(pinned OpenROAD 26Q2): \textbf{setup WNS −727.37 ps / TNS −60 665 ps /
781 violators; hold CLEAN (0 violators, WS +31.26 ps); 283.6 mW total /
89.6 mW fabric-only; 166 811 µm² / 66.80 \% util}; annotation 3 156
unannotated drivers / 0 partial, all \code{clkload<N>/Y} CTS dummy loads
plus 83 \code{u_cpu} macro debug pins --- \textbf{zero real signal
drivers unannotated}. Progression across the three fixed-flow runs:
−1144.5 ps / 40 192 violators (\code{RUN_2026-09-16_19-19-45}, bead
\bead{rvb} Path A/B fixes) → −1050.2 ps / 33 196
(\code{RUN_2026-09-17_09-01-42}, bead \bead{ydw} registered SRAM read) →
\textbf{−727.4 ps / 781} (\code{RUN_2026-09-17_20-41-11}, bead
\bead{rvb} write pipeline).

\textbf{Scope of this update --- what it does and does not validate:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Validated}: the ASAP7 SoC on \emph{current} RTL, on the fixed
  flow, is measured honestly. Timing is \emph{still not closed} at 1750
  ps (−727 ps, 781 endpoints, all internal to the SRAM
  array\textquotesingle s own bounded group fan-out).
\item
  \textbf{NOT validated, unchanged by this update}: run 14 (571 MHz /
  62.9 mW), run 23 (both domains met), CPU run 43 (1418 MHz), CPU M7
  (1282 MHz) and the GPU block (571 MHz / 262 mW). Their artifacts were
  wiped (bead \bead{alq}) and their RTL vintage differs, so they cannot
  be re-measured --- they stay unvalidated-not-confirmed-wrong. GPU
  re-validation remains deferred to bead \code{claude_verilog_test-2kh}
  (host-gated on \code{2kn}).
\item
  \textbf{Unaffected by this update}: the zero-routed-wire caveat
  (\code{xy6}) and the macro-power-excluded caveat
  (\code{86a}/\code{ew3}) both still stand --- these figures use
  annotated GRT parasitics, not SPEF, and exclude both hard macros.
\end{itemize}
\end{quote}

\end{record}

\subsubsection{PDN note (why the tap-cell violations are accepted)}\label{cr:run:phase5:pdn-note-why-the-tap-cell-violations-are-accepted}

\begin{record}

The 8.28 M power-grid violations are all
\texttt{PSM-\allowbreak{}0039\ Unconnected\ instance\ TAP\_\allowbreak{}TAPCELL\_\allowbreak{}ROW\_\allowbreak{}*/\allowbreak{}VDD|VSS}
--- a documented ASAP7 tool artifact (tap-cell wells tie through the
substrate, not the routed grid). The standalone CPU/GPU ASAP7 sign-offs
carried \textasciitilde1.8 M of the identical artifact and were
accepted. Real std-cell + macro power connectivity is intact
(timing/\allowbreak{}power are valid). This is an \textbf{indicative
ASAP7} sign-off (predictive PDK --- no Magic/\allowbreak{}KLayout DRC or
Netgen LVS, as with the CPU/GPU sign-offs).

\end{record}

\subsubsection{Caveats --- read before quoting any number above}\label{cr:run:phase5:caveats-read-before-quoting-any-number-above}

\begin{record}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{The power comparison is not like-for-like --- and is not a
  real SoC total in the first place.} \code{report_power} attributes
  \textbf{0.00 W to the macros}, and the drop from 62.9 mW coincides
  with the first-ever insertion of real ICG clock gates
  (\code{USE_ICG_CELL}), not with the domain split. Do not claim
  multi-clock as a power win on this evidence. Full root-cause
  investigation, independent re-measurement, and how to bound the true
  SoC power → \textbf{§ Power figure caveat,
  below (\cref{cr:run:phase5:power-figure-caveat-macro-power-omitted-from-every-figure-bead-ew3})}.
\item
  \textbf{\code{cpu_clk} covers 960 flat registers}, not the CPU core
  --- the core is a hard macro whose interior is fixed by its own
  sign-off (which closes at −0.87 ps setup / +8.54 ps hold). The 960 are
  the CDC bridge\textquotesingle s CPU face, the synchronisers, the
  clock gate and the PLL2 APB registers.
\item
  \textbf{Optimisation guard band was \textasciitilde0 before bead
  \bead{s9f}} --- the resizer margins were declared
  \texttt{units=\allowbreak{}"ns"} but read in the
  Liberty\textquotesingle s \code{1ps}. Run 23 is the first multi-clock
  run with real margins --- 45 ps setup / 25 ps hold in
  \src{pnr/asap7/soc/config_multiclock.json}; the CPU block config
  (\src{pnr/asap7/cpu/config.json}) uses 20 ps / 10 ps, sized under its
  own achieved slack.
\item
  \textbf{IR drop is still unobtainable}: \code{analyze_power_grid}
  fails \code{PSM-0069} on both rails from the same M1-only tap-cell
  connectivity artifact.
\item
  Indicative ASAP7 (predictive PDK): no Magic/\allowbreak{}KLayout DRC,
  no Netgen LVS.
\end{itemize}

\end{record}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{How \code{cpu_clk} was closed --- two
independent problems}{How  was closed --- two independent problems}}\label{cr:run:phase5:how-cpu-clk-was-closed-two-independent-problems}

\begin{record}

\textbf{1. The macro exported \textasciitilde600--800 ps combinational
in→out arcs on its APB outputs.} \code{apb_prdata_o},
\code{apb_pslverr_o} and \code{apb_pready_o} are now pure registered
outputs; no output pin of the macro carries a combinational arc. Two
earlier attempts failed and are recorded in \code{rv32i_cpu_top.sv} so
they are not retried: registering \code{prdata} alone merely
\textbf{relocated} the delay into an 876.85 ps \emph{input setup
requirement}, and a SETUP-phase decode for \code{pslverr} still left a
617.9 ps arc re-sourced from \code{apb_psel_i}. Every APB output pays
\textasciitilde600 ps crossing this block\textquotesingle s internal
distribution regardless of logic depth, so only a pure flop breaks it.

\textbf{2. The macro boundary was not reproducible.}
\src{pnr/asap7/cpu/config.json} had no \code{FP_PIN_ORDER_CFG}, so each
regeneration free-floated the pins --- \textbf{393 of 401 moved} between
two consecutive builds. In run 22 that perturbed SoC placement around
\code{u_cpu}, doubled post-CTS \code{cpu_clk} skew (−485.6 → −922.1 ps),
drove hold violations 805 → 4702 endpoints and 4814 inserted hold
buffers, and took \code{cpu_clk} to −596.0 ps. With \code{pin_order.cfg}
two runs now produce a byte-identical LEF (0/401 pins moved), which made
tuning measurable for the first time --- block slack had previously
drawn randomly from a \textasciitilde47 ps band.

Tuning then took two measured iterations, using the SoC context
(\code{u_cpu} sits at 365,15, so W/N face the fabric and E/S face the
die edge):

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)*457/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)*174/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)*185/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)*163/1000\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Pin-order revision
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Block setup WS
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Block setup TNS
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Block hold WS
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
v1 --- replicate the free-floated sides & −25.87 ps & −1180.02 & +27.47
ps \\
v2 --- group AXI onto W/N & −10.48 ps & −10.48 & −2.06 ps \\
v3 --- move APB off the fabric-facing edge & \textbf{−0.87 ps} &
\textbf{−0.87} & \textbf{+8.54 ps} \\
\end{longtable}
}

v2\textquotesingle s hold violator was \code{apb_paddr_i[*]}, which
pointed straight at the error: APB carries a 3-cycle setup multicycle at
block level, so those paths want to be \textbf{long} and should never
occupy scarce fabric-facing slots.

\end{record}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{What made \code{cpu_clk} hard (measured,
not inferred)}{What made  hard (measured, not inferred)}}\label{cr:run:phase5:what-made-cpu-clk-hard-measured-not-inferred}

\begin{record}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Status note.} This subsection records the runs 16--19 diagnosis.
§10a is \textbf{retired} in the accepted run-23 configuration: all three
APB outputs are now pure registered outputs, so the combinational arc it
excluded no longer exists. What remains active is §10b (the APB
\emph{input} multicycle mirroring \code{asap7.sdc:183-184}). See "How
\code{cpu_clk} was closed" above for the shipped state.
\end{quote}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{An 800.71 ps combinational arc inside the CPU macro} ---
  \code{apb_paddr_i} → \code{apb_prdata_o}, the APB debug read mux
  (\code{rv32i_cpu_top.sv:261-285}, pure \code{always_comb}). It cannot
  fit a 780 ps period and is fixed in Liberty. The CPU\textquotesingle s
  own standalone sign-off already excludes it
  (\texttt{set\_\allowbreak{}false\_\allowbreak{}path\ -\allowbreak{}to\ [get\_\allowbreak{}ports\ apb\_\allowbreak{}prdata\_\allowbreak{}o[*]]},
  \src{pnr/asap7/cpu/constraints/asap7.sdc:150}), so SDC §10a restores
  that contract at SoC level. Root fix → bead \bead{cyb}. Note
  \code{-through}, not \code{-from}: at block level the pin is an
  endpoint, at SoC level the path continues through it.
\item
  \textbf{Hold padding eating setup.} Before run 19, 161 of 162
  violating paths ran through resizer-inserted \code{HB4xp67} hold
  buffers, chains up to 20 deep --- one path spent 665 ps of its 1050 ps
  arrival in 9 buffers while its real logic was \textasciitilde222 ps.
  Driver: \code{cpu_clk} insertion-delay imbalance (109.3 ps launch vs
  256.8 ps capture). Tighter CTS clustering addressed part of it.
\end{enumerate}

\end{record}

\subsection{The synthesis blocker and fix (the central M11 problem)}\label{cr:run:phase5:the-synthesis-blocker-and-fix-the-central-m11-problem}

\begin{record}

LibreLane\textquotesingle s \textbf{Synlig/\allowbreak{}UHDM
SystemVerilog frontend cannot synthesize this SoC}:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \code{SYNLIG_DEFER=true} --- silently drops peripheral FSM logic (0
  DFFs → hollow P\&R).
\item
  \code{SYNLIG_DEFER=false} --- crashes Yosys \code{rtlil.cc:2150}
  (empty wire name in UHDM \code{-link}).
\item
  \code{USE_SYNLIG=false} --- native
  \texttt{read\_\allowbreak{}verilog\ -\allowbreak{}sv} fails on
  \texttt{package…endpackage}.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Fix:}
\texttt{sv2v\ 0.\allowbreak{}0.\allowbreak{}13.\allowbreak{}1} (nixpkgs
\code{haskellPackages.sv2v}) converts all SoC SystemVerilog → flat
Verilog-2005, then native Yosys \code{read_verilog} + \code{flatten} +
blackbox the two macros → 43,546 DFFs, 110 k cells, clean. The verified
RTL (\code{soc_all} 82/82 as of M11) remains the correctness source;
sv2v is a synth-frontend transform only (Verilator lint skipped on its
benign \texttt{1'sb0}/\allowbreak{}ascending-range output).
\textbf{Always sanity-check DFF count \textgreater{} 0 after SoC synth.}
Core commit \code{6ee91b6}.

Also fixed en route: SoC-level SDC (single \code{clk_i} clock + clean
H-tree), \code{PDN_MACRO_CONNECTIONS} (removed the behavioral
\code{sram_controller}, which is flat std-cells, not a macro), die
shrink (720→520 µm), and the librelane tool patches (\code{pyosys.py}
json\_\allowbreak{}header, \code{synthesize.py} hierarchy \code{-check})
needed for the flow.

\end{record}

\subsection{Reproduce}\label{cr:run:phase5:reproduce}

\begin{record}

\begin{lstlisting}[language=bash]
cd pnr && make librelane-asap7-soc      # sv2v frontend; run dir on /nobackup
# metrics: <run>/final/metrics.json
\end{lstlisting}

\end{record}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{Power figure caveat: macro power omitted
from every figure (bead
\code{ew3})}{Power figure caveat: macro power omitted from every figure (bead )}}\label{cr:run:phase5:power-figure-caveat-macro-power-omitted-from-every-figure-bead-ew3}

\begin{record}

Filed after \ghref{96} (run 23 vs. run 14) surfaced
\texttt{Macro\ =\allowbreak{}\ 0.\allowbreak{}00\ W} in
\code{report_power} for both hard macros. Investigated 2026-08-15; bead
\code{claude_verilog_test-ew3}. This section is the authoritative
statement --- every inline \warn{} mark above points here.

\begin{quote}
\textbf{UPDATE 2026-09-09 (bead \bead{86a}) --- the macros are now
characterized. The figures in this document remain fabric-only and are
NOT retro-edited; the macro-inclusive numbers below are NEW and carry
different assumptions.}

\src{pnr/scripts/asap7_macro_power.tcl} +
\code{asap7_macro_power_inject.py} (commit \code{7b7dbae}) run
\code{report_power} on each macro\textquotesingle s own gate-level
netlist and inject \code{cell_leakage_power} / \code{internal_power}
into the tracked macro Liberty. Round- tripped against run
23\textquotesingle s signed-off flat netlist, the \textbf{Macro row goes
from 0.00 W to 222.545 mW} (internal 217.089 + leakage 5.456), for a
whole-SoC total of \textbf{306.264 mW}.

\textbf{Read those two numbers differently:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{306.264 mW} is the cleaner figure --- one methodology,
  everything at the same flat 0.20/0.5 activity.
\item
  \textbf{51.9 mW (fabric, run 23) + 222.5 mW (macros) ≈ 274.4 mW} MIXES
  methodologies: real post-route parasitics on the fabric, flat
  vectorless activity and no wire RC on the macros. Quote it only with
  that caveat, never as a measured total.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Two assumptions, both stacked, both disclosed in the generated
\code{.lib}:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Flat vectorless activity
  (\texttt{set\_\allowbreak{}power\_\allowbreak{}activity\ -\allowbreak{}global\ -\allowbreak{}activity\ 0.\allowbreak{}20\ -\allowbreak{}duty\ 0.\allowbreak{}5})
  --- this repo\textquotesingle s own existing convention from
  \src{pnr/scripts/08_power.tcl}, not invented for the task. No VCD or
  SAIF exists for either macro.
\item
  No post-route SPEF/DEF was available (the CPU run directories had been
  pruned), so this is Liberty-table power from gate input-pin
  capacitance only, with \textbf{no wire RC}.
\end{enumerate}

Magnitude was sanity-checked rather than assumed: CPU macro leakage
matches 10 × 128.9 µW of real vendor SRAM Liberty leakage to five
digits, and the Liberty \code{internal_power} scalar unit was derived
\emph{empirically} with a single-cell test library
(\texttt{capacitive\_\allowbreak{}load\_\allowbreak{}unit\ ×\ voltage\_\allowbreak{}unit²}
= 1 fJ), not guessed.

Still outstanding before \code{86a} closes: re-run SoC
\code{report_power} inside a real flow rather than the standalone
round-trip. Do \textbf{not} use the LibreLane 3.0.14 shell for that ---
its \code{sta} is OpenSTA 2.7.0, which corrupts \code{report_power} to
inf/NaN on this design (bead \bead{zwc}).

\textbf{UPDATE 2026-09-16 (bead \bead{86a} closing) --- in-flow figure
on CURRENT RTL, honestly annotated, from bead
\bead{w3a}\textquotesingle s post-GRT STA close-out. NOT a replacement
for any number above --- those stay as recorded, on their own RTL
vintage and methodology.}

Run \code{w3a-sta-7} (\code{RUN_2026-09-16_05-52-48}, step
\code{01-openroad-stamidpnr-3}),
\code{config_multiclock_hier_rsz7.json},
\code{STA_POSTGRT_INSESSION_GRT=true} (bead \bead{8f3}) so the
parasitics are a real in-session global-route annotation, not a
stale/\allowbreak{}persisted-guide read. Annotation proven: \textbf{1553
unannotated drivers, 0 partial, of \textasciitilde213 464 total
(≈0.7\%)} --- below the CPU block\textquotesingle s own 1.71\% validated
floor, vs. 213 464 (essentially all of them) unannotated on the same
design under the unpatched stock STA step. Only quote the numbers below
because that gate passed; if it had not, they would be worthless the
same way \code{w3a-rsz-5}\textquotesingle s now-withdrawn
\texttt{-\allowbreak{}426.\allowbreak{}262\ ps} / clean-hold figure was.

\textbf{Fabric + macro power split}, \code{report_power} at
\code{nom_tt_025C_0p7V}:

{

& 100\% \\

}

\textbf{Vectorless-activity caveat applies exactly as before}
(\texttt{set\_\allowbreak{}power\_\allowbreak{}activity\ -\allowbreak{}global\ -\allowbreak{}activity\ 0.\allowbreak{}20\ -\allowbreak{}duty\ 0.\allowbreak{}5},
this repo\textquotesingle s own convention, no VCD/SAIF for either
macro): the Macro figure (167.331 mW) lands almost exactly on the
previously-established \textbf{CPU-domain-gated lower bound (167.3 mW)}
from this bead\textquotesingle s own earlier operating-point sweep, not
the default-vectorless midpoint (177.4 mW) or the all-domains-on upper
bound (222.5 mW, using the standalone CPU block\textquotesingle s 56.5
mW as the only available full-activity CPU proxy). Mechanism unchanged
from the original finding: under default vectorless STA activity,
\code{u_gpu_cg.u_icg}\textquotesingle s enable resolves to a
\emph{provable constant} (GPU reproduces its full standalone dynamic
power), while \code{u_cpu_cg.u_icg}\textquotesingle s enable is real
PMU→CDC-synchroniser logic that gets derated to \textasciitilde18\%
duty, so the CPU macro\textquotesingle s \emph{dynamic} power is
\textasciitilde0 here and only its leakage (1.29 mW of the 5.456 mW
macro leakage total) shows up. \textbf{Report the range, not a single
number}: 167.3 mW (CPU-gated, what this run actually measured) .. 177.4
mW (default average) .. 222.5 mW (all domains on) for the macro
contribution alone.

\textbf{This is NOT comparable to run 23\textquotesingle s 51.9 mW
fabric-only figure or the 306.264 mW standalone round-trip above} ---
different RTL vintage (current RTL is
post-\code{rfz}/\allowbreak{}post-CDC-hardening; run 23 predates those
changes), different PD flow (deferred\_\allowbreak{}flatten + the
\code{w3a} CTS/\allowbreak{}repair fixes, vs run 23\textquotesingle s
flat synthesis), and a genuinely in-flow, honestly-annotated measurement
rather than a standalone round-trip or a mixed-methodology sum. Do not
average or combine it with the older figures.

\textbf{Timing is NOT closed at this checkpoint} (tracked in beads
\code{w3a}, \code{rvb}, and \code{0ah} --- not a power-bead concern,
noted here only so this power figure is not read as implying a
signed-off design): setup WS −2084.08 ps / TNS −2.19 × 10⁷ ps / 41 864
violators, hold WS −917.23 ps / TNS −392 273 ps / 718 violators.

\ok{} \textbf{Superseded 2026-09-18 (bead \bead{0d0})}: those numbers
are the 2026-09-16 \code{w3a-sta-7} snapshot, which was additionally
captured \emph{before} \code{ResizerTimingPostGRT} ran (see bead
\bead{0ah}). Three later full fixed-flow runs supersede them; current
basis \code{RUN_2026-09-17_20-41-11}: setup WNS \textbf{−727.37 ps} /
TNS −60 665 ps / \textbf{781} violators, hold \textbf{CLEAN},
\textbf{283.6 mW} total / 89.6 mW fabric-only. Timing is still not
closed. See the dated 2026-09-18 update block near the top of this
document for the full scope of what that does and does not validate.

\textbf{Bead \code{0p6} note}: \texttt{run\ 23}\textquotesingle s
historical post-GRT timing figures elsewhere in this document (and by
extension any power figure derived from run 23\textquotesingle s
post-GRT state) were measured with the SAME stock, non-re-routing STA
step that bead \bead{8f3} proved gives false-clean results on this
design (94.44\% unannotated on an isolated CPU-block test). They have
\textbf{not} been re-validated with \code{STA_POSTGRT_INSESSION_GRT=1}
and must be treated as \textbf{unvalidated, not confirmed-wrong} ---
re-validation is bead \bead{0p6}\textquotesingle s own open scope, not
retro-edited here.
\end{quote}

\textbf{Finding 1 --- the macro Liberty files have no power tables at
all, by construction.}
\texttt{pnr/\allowbreak{}asap7/\allowbreak{}\{cpu,\allowbreak{}gpu\}/\allowbreak{}macro/\allowbreak{}*\_\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}nom\_\allowbreak{}tt\_\allowbreak{}025C\_\allowbreak{}0p7V.\allowbreak{}lib}
(the tracked source of truth; \code{pnr/asap7/soc/macro/*} are
gitignored copies of the same content) each declare
\texttt{leakage\_\allowbreak{}power\_\allowbreak{}unit\ :\allowbreak{}\ 1pW;}
and never use it --- a full-text grep of both files for
\code{leakage_power}, \code{cell_leakage_power}, and
\code{internal_power} finds \textbf{zero} occurrences beyond that one
unit declaration. Every \code{cell()} / \code{pin()} block carries only
\code{capacitance} and \code{timing()} (setup/\allowbreak{}hold
constraints, \code{cell_rise}/\code{cell_fall}/\code{*_transition}
tables) --- no \code{power()} group of any kind. \code{report_power} is
not misreading these files; the files contain nothing to report.

\textbf{Finding 2 --- this is a characterization-flow gap, not an
OpenROAD registration or corner-mapping bug.} Both macro libs generate
from \src{pnr/scripts/asap7_macro_views.tcl}, which after loading the
routed macro ODB and the real stdcell/\allowbreak{}SRAM Liberty set
calls a single OpenSTA/\allowbreak{}OpenROAD command,
\code{write_timing_model}, to emit the abstracted \code{.lib}. Tracing
that command to its implementation (\code{MakeTimingModel.cc} in the
vendored OpenSTA source,
\code{/nix/store/.../src/sta/search/MakeTimingModel.cc}) shows
\textbf{zero power-related code} --- a case-insensitive grep for
\code{power} in the file returns nothing. \code{write_timing_model}
characterizes I/O timing arcs only; it has no code path that could emit
\code{cell_leakage_power} or \code{internal_power} even in principle.
The fix, if pursued, belongs in the macro characterization step
(\code{asap7_macro_views.tcl} would need a genuinely different
tool/\allowbreak{}technique --- e.g. running \code{report_power} on the
macro\textquotesingle s own signed-off block netlist under
representative activity and hand-injecting the resulting lumped numbers
as \code{cell_leakage_power} / pin \code{internal_power} --- not a flag
or option that exists on \code{write_timing_model} today), \textbf{not}
in how the SoC flow loads or maps the resulting \code{.lib}.

The SoC-level registration was checked and is correct:
\src{pnr/asap7/soc/config.json} lists both macro libs under
\code{EXTRA_LIBS} in the same \code{nom_tt_025C_0p7V} corner slot as the
stdcell/\allowbreak{}SRAM Liberty (\code{config.json:100-104}) --- there
is no separate "macro corner" mapping to get wrong.

\textbf{Finding 3 --- independently re-measured, reproduces exactly.}
Using the \code{eda-openroad} MCP session server (\code{load_odb} on run
23\textquotesingle s final ODB,
\code{pnr/asap7/soc/runs/RUN_2026-08-09_14-36-16/final/odb/soc_top.odb},
with the 5 stdcell Liberty files + SRAM Liberty + \textbf{both} macro
Liberty files explicitly loaded, then \code{get_power}) reproduces the
archived figure bit-for-bit:

\begin{lstlisting}[language={}]
Group          Internal    Switching    Leakage       Total
Sequential     3.02e-02    4.65e-05     9.62e-06      3.02e-02   58.2%
Combinational  2.41e-04    2.65e-04     2.34e-05      5.30e-04    1.0%
Clock          1.31e-02    8.04e-03     1.73e-06      2.12e-02   40.8%
Macro          0.00e+00    0.00e+00     0.00e+00      0.00e+00    0.0%
Pad            0.00e+00    0.00e+00     0.00e+00      0.00e+00    0.0%
Total          4.35e-02    8.36e-03     3.48e-05      5.19e-02  100.0%
\end{lstlisting}

Total 51.9134 mW, matching \code{final/metrics.json}\textquotesingle s
\code{power__total} exactly. This rules out "the archived run just
didn\textquotesingle t have the macro libs loaded" as an explanation ---
the libs were loaded, correctly, and still contribute nothing, because
there is nothing in them to contribute. (Unrelated: bead \bead{pzj}, a
known \code{get_area} limitation of the same MCP server, was not touched
--- different workstream.)

\textbf{Per-hierarchy comparison, run 18 vs. run 23} (both fabric-only;
the macro row is 0.00 W / 0.0\% in both, by the same mechanism):

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}lll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Group & Run 18 & Run 23 \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
Sequential & 29.9 mW (69.0\%) & 30.2 mW (58.2\%) \\
Clock & 13.0 mW (30.0\%) & 21.2 mW (40.8\%) \\
Combinational & 0.44 mW (1.0\%) & 0.53 mW (1.0\%) \\
Macro & 0.00 mW (0\%) & 0.00 mW (0\%) \\
\textbf{Total} & \textbf{43.3 mW} & \textbf{51.9 mW} \\
\end{longtable}
}

Clock power\textquotesingle s share nearly doubling (30.0\% → 40.8\%)
while total power \emph{rose} between run 18 and run 23 is consistent
with the ICG-insertion timeline (\code{USE_ICG_CELL} landed in
\code{SOC_SV2V_DEFINES} during \ghref{96} Gate B, between these two runs)
changing where switching activity is attributed, not with any claim
about macro contribution --- the macro row is identically absent in
both.

\textbf{Bounding the true gap.} The standalone block sign-offs \emph{do}
include their own internals, because they run \code{report_power} on the
flat block netlist directly rather than through a
\code{write_timing_model} abstraction:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  CPU standalone (\src{pnr/asap7/cpu/}, M7-updated 780 ps sign-off):
  \textbf{24.38 mW}
\item
  GPU standalone (\src{pnr/asap7/gpu/}): \textbf{262 mW}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Naively summing these onto the 51.9 mW SoC figure (→
\textasciitilde338 mW) is the wrong arithmetic and must not be quoted as
an estimate of true SoC power.} The block sign-offs use
\code{report_power}\textquotesingle s default/\allowbreak{}no-VCD
activity assumptions against each block\textquotesingle s \emph{own}
clock and I/O environment in isolation (the GPU figure in particular
reflects the GPU\textquotesingle s own 571 MHz sign-off activity, not
its activity as driven by the SoC\textquotesingle s crossbar and DMA in
context); the SoC run applies the same default-activity mechanism to the
fabric only, under the SoC\textquotesingle s own clocking. Summing
conflates two different activity contexts and double-uses the same
default-activity assumption rather than compounding a real one. All
three numbers can be reported side by side as bounds --- a true
macro-inclusive SoC total is almost certainly between the fabric-only
51.9 mW and the naive-sum \textasciitilde338 mW, closer to the sum given
the macros\textquotesingle{} area/\allowbreak{}gate-count dominance ---
but no arithmetic on the numbers available in this repo produces a
defensible point estimate.

\textbf{Conclusion --- this cannot be fixed inside OpenROAD or this
project\textquotesingle s SoC PD flow.} The macro \code{.lib} files
never carried power tables because \code{write_timing_model}
doesn\textquotesingle t characterize power; no \code{EXTRA_LIBS}/corner
re-mapping in \code{config.json} changes that. A real fix requires a
macro power characterization step that does not exist in this repo
today. Filed as a follow-up: \textbf{bead \code{claude_verilog_test-ew3}
is closed} with this investigation as the resolution (root cause
identified, re-measurement confirmed, gap bounded, docs corrected); the
actual fix --- adding a macro power characterization step to
\code{asap7_macro_views.tcl} (or equivalent) so
\code{rv32i_cpu_top}/\code{gpu_top} carry real
\code{cell_leakage_power}/\code{internal_power} tables --- is tracked
separately as \textbf{bead \code{claude_verilog_test-86a}} so it
isn\textquotesingle t lost. Any future SoC power number must carry the
same \warn{} fabric-only caveat until that follow-up lands.

\end{record}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{Routing / physical-closure caveat: detailed
routing committed zero wires (bead
\code{xy6})}{Routing / physical-closure caveat: detailed routing committed zero wires (bead )}}\label{cr:run:phase5:routing-physical-closure-caveat-detailed-routing-committed-zero-wires-bead-xy6}

\begin{record}

Found 2026-08-16 while standalone-validating the RCX ruleset for bead
\bead{e69} against run 23\textquotesingle s ODB. \textbf{MEASURED, not
inferred}, and swept across every ASAP7 run with recoverable artifacts
on disk (SoC, GPU, and CPU alike) as part of root-causing bead
\code{claude_verilog_test-xy6}. This section is the authoritative
statement --- every inline \warn{} mark above points here.

\begin{quote}
\textbf{RESOLVED for new runs, 2026-09-09 (bead \bead{ocm}). This
section\textquotesingle s findings about runs 14, 23 and every other
pre-September run REMAIN TRUE and are not rehabilitated --- those runs
really did commit zero wires.} What changed is that ASAP7 detailed
routing now works at all.

Root cause: LibreLane 2.4.13\textquotesingle s \emph{own pinned}
OpenROAD (\code{edf00dff}) cannot grant pin access to \textbf{any} ASAP7
top-level pin --- 401/401 BTerms fail --- while passing Sky130 cleanly
on the identical probe. LibreLane 3.0.14\textquotesingle s pin
(\code{dcf36133}) resolves it. A residual 16 \code{DRT-0255} were traced
to a single macro column and cleared by a +10 DBU shift on
\code{gen_data_sram[1]}.

First passing run: \textbf{\code{RUN_2026-09-09_14-56-03}},
\src{tools/verif/check_asap7_routing.py} exit 0 --- \textbf{33,396
routed net records}, \code{route__wirelength} 270,938,
\code{route__wirelength__max} 321.17 (0 on every previous run ever
recorded), \code{drt_error_count} 0, 52 steps including
\code{write_views}. Marked \code{.signoff} so \code{prune_runs} cannot
evict it.

\warn{} \textbf{This is NOT physical closure and must not be quoted as
one.}
\texttt{route\_\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}drc\_\allowbreak{}errors\ =\allowbreak{}\ 2045}
on that run, because \code{DRT_OPT_ITERS} was capped at 12 to fit the
host\textquotesingle s time and memory; the gate deliberately does
\textbf{not} check DRC violations. The residual, triaged by rule: 1135
M3.Lef58EolKeepOut, 560 M2 Metal Spacing, 215 M3.Short, 81 V2 Cut
Spacing, 33 V3.Lef58CutSpacingTable, 12 M2.Lef58SpacingEndOfLine, 5 V2
Cut Short, 4 M6 Rect Only --- i.e. 220 real shorts, the rest
spacing/\allowbreak{}keepout. An unbounded run reached 43 violations by
iteration \textasciitilde45 but peaked at 13 GiB on a 15.9 GB host and
died before \code{write_views}.

\warn{} \textbf{The point fix is a per-instance workaround, not a rule.}
Its positive control --- moving a \emph{passing} column onto the
identical near-grid residue class --- did \textbf{not} reproduce the
failure, so grid alignment is necessary-looking but demonstrably not
sufficient. Any re-placement, die-size or macro-count change can drop a
different column into the same condition.

Consequence for \code{e69}: the first genuine post-route parasitic
extraction in this project now exists --- a 61,048,456-byte SPEF
consumed by \code{OpenROAD.STAPostPNR} --- for the \textbf{CPU block
only}. GPU and SoC still have \code{RUN_SPEF_EXTRACTION} false.
\end{quote}

\textbf{What "zero wires" means, precisely.}
\code{OpenROAD.DetailedRouting}\textquotesingle s \code{detailed_route}
call is wrapped in \texttt{catch\ \{\}} by a local (uncommitted-to-
upstream) patch to LibreLane\textquotesingle s
\code{librelane/scripts/openroad/drt.tcl}. On every run below, the
pin-access phase
(\texttt{[INFO\ DRT-\allowbreak{}0165]\ Start\ pin\ access}) hits an
unresolved access point --- \texttt{[ERROR\ DRT-\allowbreak{}0073]} for
a macro boundary pin (SoC integration, 1--2 pins) or
\texttt{[ERROR\ DRT-\allowbreak{}0074]} for a top-level I/O pin
(standalone CPU/GPU blocks, hundreds of pins) --- \textbf{before track
assignment or any search-and-repair pass ever runs.} The Tcl error is
caught, a
\texttt{WARNING:\allowbreak{}\ detailed\_\allowbreak{}route\ reported\ errors;\ continuing}
line is printed, and \code{write_views} commits the DEF as-is:
\code{final/def/*.def} has \textbf{zero} \texttt{ROUTED\ } net records,
\code{final/metrics.json}\textquotesingle s
\code{route__wirelength__max} is \code{0}, and every downstream checker
(\code{design__violations}, antenna, max slew/cap) reports 0 because
nothing was routed to violate. Reproduction:
\texttt{grep\ -\allowbreak{}c\ 'ROUTED\ '\ <run>/\allowbreak{}final/\allowbreak{}def/\allowbreak{}*.\allowbreak{}def}.

\textbf{Scope: this is not a regression, and not limited to the SoC.}
The leading hypothesis when this was filed was that the run-23-era
changes (registering the CPU macro\textquotesingle s APB outputs, bead
\bead{cyb}/\code{g0o}; adding \code{pin_order.cfg}, bead \ghref{96})
introduced the failure. They did not --- the identical zero-wire pattern
is present on \textbf{every} ASAP7 run with recoverable final-stage
artifacts, including the GPU 571 MHz sign-off runs from
2026-05-27/\allowbreak{}28, more than three weeks before those changes
landed. \code{drt.tcl}\textquotesingle s catch-and-continue patch itself
predates all of them: \src{memory/pd/knowledge.md}\textquotesingle s
DRT-0074 note already describes it as in place during the Phase-3 CPU
PPA campaign (runs 6--7, 2026-04-25/\allowbreak{}26), where 374 DRT-0074
errors per run were already being treated as "structural, non-fatal"
without anyone connecting that acceptance to "therefore zero wires were
committed." \textbf{No ASAP7 run inspected in this investigation ever
produced a genuinely routed design.}

Runs not listed either predate the artifact retention window
(\code{/nobackup} periodically cleaned; see bead \bead{o1i} on host
reboots) or never reached \code{write_views} (\code{ndef=0} in the sweep
--- earlier "hollow" synthesis-only runs 1--12,
\textasciitilde2026-06-23 06:xx). Notably absent and \textbf{not
directly re-verifiable}: the CPU 1418 MHz Run 43 (2026-05-20) and the
1282 MHz M7 re-sign-off (2026-06-02) cited in \src{CLAUDE.md} --- both
run directories are gone from disk. Given
\code{drt.tcl}\textquotesingle s catch patch was already in place by the
April Phase-3 PPA campaign (predating both), and given \textbf{100 \% of
the runs that could be checked show the same pattern with no exception},
the working assumption must be that those two are affected as well, not
that they are somehow exempt --- but this is inference, not measurement,
and is flagged as such.

\textbf{Root cause, to the depth reached.} Two separate things compound:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \emph{Software defect (root-caused, fixed by this bead --- see the
  routing gate below):} \code{drt.tcl}\textquotesingle s
  \texttt{catch\ \{\}} wraps the \textbf{entire} \code{detailed_route}
  call, not just per-pin access-point resolution. A single unresolved
  pin anywhere in a 150,000--250,000-pin design aborts the whole call
  before any wire is committed. LibreLane\textquotesingle s own upstream
  default (no catch at all) would have failed these runs loudly at step
  38/40/41 instead of completing 20+ downstream steps against an empty
  layout.
\item
  \emph{Why individual pins fail access-point resolution at all (only
  partially characterized, not fully solved):} for standalone-block
  top-level I/O pins (\code{DRT-0074}), \src{memory/pd/knowledge.md}
  already documents that ASAP7 M4/M5\textquotesingle s \code{WIDTHTABLE}
  rejects the default pin width and that even the mitigated width
  (\code{FP_IO_HTHICKNESS_MULT=5}, later 8) does not eliminate all
  failures --- congestion/\allowbreak{}obstruction near specific
  die-edge locations is the untested remaining hypothesis. For SoC
  macro-boundary pins (\code{DRT-0073}), the failing pins are on the
  CPU/GPU hard-macro abstract LEFs (e.g. \code{apb_paddr_i[5]} at
  \texttt{RECT\ 126\ 15.\allowbreak{}042\ 130\ 15.\allowbreak{}234} on
  M4 in the current \code{rv32i_cpu_top.lef}) --- the exact geometry in
  force \emph{at run-14 time} cannot be recovered
  (pin\_\allowbreak{}order.cfg postdates run 14 by \ghref{96}, and 393/401
  boundary pins reshuffle on every macro regeneration absent it), so the
  precise geometric cause for run 14\textquotesingle s two specific pins
  is unconfirmed. The \code{gyx} PSM-0069 tap-cell connectivity artifact
  was considered as a related mechanism (both are
  PDK/\allowbreak{}geometry artifacts on the ASAP7 predictive stack) but
  no evidence ties the two together --- they stay separate beads, not
  merged.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{What survives and what doesn\textquotesingle t.} The
setup/\allowbreak{}hold WNS/TNS numbers quoted throughout this document
are \textbf{not fabricated} --- \code{OpenROAD.STAPostPNR} genuinely
runs and genuinely computes timing on the placed netlist. But because
detailed routing committed no wires, the parasitics behind every one of
those numbers are the \textbf{pre-route global-route (GRT) estimate},
not real extracted post-route RC --- STAPostPNR runs after
\code{DetailedRouting} in flow order, but "after DetailedRouting" and
"on routed parasitics" are not the same thing when DetailedRouting
produced nothing. Read plainly:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Survives, self-consistent on its own terms:}
  setup/\allowbreak{}hold WNS/TNS, fmax, cell count, utilization, die
  area, std-cell power (all computed from GRT-estimated RC on a real
  placed netlist --- this was already true before this investigation and
  is a pre-existing, separately documented limitation, not new
  information).
\item
  \textbf{Does not survive --- withdrawn as of this bead:} every
  "Routing DRC 0", "antenna 0 violating nets/\allowbreak{}pins", and "0
  max slew/\allowbreak{}cap/\allowbreak{}fanout" claim in this document,
  for every run in the table above, including run 14 and run 23. These
  numbers are not "clean" --- they are computed over an empty routed
  layout and are vacuously zero.
\item
  \textbf{Downstream consequence:} bead \code{claude_verilog_test-e69}
  (RCX ruleset) is effectively blocked behind this bead --- RCX
  extraction on an unrouted design cannot produce a meaningful SPEF.
  Cross-linked, not merged.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Fix landed with this bead.}
\src{tools/verif/check_asap7_routing.py} + \src{pnr/Makefile} targets
\code{check-asap7-routing} / \code{check-asap7-gpu-routing} /
\code{check-asap7-soc-routing}, wired as a mandatory gate immediately
after every full-flow ASAP7 \code{librelane-asap7*} target (before
pruning, so a failing run is never pruned away) --- the build now fails
loudly (non-zero exit) whenever a completed run has zero routed net
records,
\texttt{route\_\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}wirelength\_\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}max\ =\allowbreak{}=\allowbreak{}\ 0},
or any unresolved \texttt{[ERROR\ DRT-\allowbreak{}*]} in the
\code{OpenROAD.DetailedRouting} log, following the precedent of bead
\bead{dwp} ("a tool exiting 0 with an empty or absent report must never
be read as PASS"). \textbf{Root-causing why the individual pin-access
points fail (item 2 above) is not solved by this bead} and is tracked as
a follow-up; no full P\&R run was launched to pursue it further given
the 2026-08-16 06:48 host reboot (bead \bead{o1i}) that had just killed
a 9-hour in-flight run.

\end{record}

\subsection{Known limitations / follow-ups}\label{cr:run:phase5:known-limitations-follow-ups}

\begin{record}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{sv2v vs source RTL --- now formally checked
  (2026-09-12/\allowbreak{}13, bead \bead{q7n}), not fully closed.}
  Originally correctness rested only on \code{soc_all} 82/82 (M11-era
  count; 120/120 as of 2026-08-01). Gold is the \textbf{source RTL read
  by yosys-slang} --- an independent frontend --- and gate is
  \code{soc_top_sv2v.v}, so this checks sv2v itself rather than a
  self-consistency.

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \textbf{Flat \code{soc_top}:} 64 209 of 64 433 equivalence points
    proven (99.65 \%), \textbf{0 disproven}. Depth is host-capped at
    \texttt{equiv\_\allowbreak{}induct\ -\allowbreak{}seq\ 5}: seq 7, 10
    and 20 all die at an identical \textasciitilde12.57 GB.
  \item
    \textbf{Per module} (\src{tools/verif/equiv_sv2v_module.sh}, sv2v
    keeps module boundaries): \textbf{25 of 27 modules PROVEN outright}
    at seq-20 induction, including \code{axi4_crossbar}, \code{soc_bus},
    \code{async_axi_fifo}; \code{sram_controller} at \code{MEM_WORDS=16}
    (its 4096-word array is unaffordable at full size; the transform is
    depth-independent). The flat miter\textquotesingle s unproven
    \code{m_araddr} group proves cleanly at module level, so it was a
    flattening artefact.
  \item
    \textbf{Remaining two, bounded evidence only:} \code{pmu} --- 39
    FSM-output points induction-unproven, but a 40-cycle
    \textbf{post-reset} bounded miter passes (39 cycles after reset ---
    enough for a full power-down→up through both
    domains\textquotesingle{} 9-state sequencers; 20 cycles was not).
    \code{dma_engine} --- 128 points
    (\code{src_q}/\code{dst_q}/\code{words_rem_q}/\code{m_araddr})
    unproven even at seq-20 induction; 8-cycle bounded miters pass both
    from the zero state and after reset.
  \item
    A PMU miter first reported a counterexample; it was a
    \textbf{pre-reset first-cycle artefact} --- identical outputs for
    all 19 post-reset cycles. Bounded miters here need reset asserted at
    step 1 \emph{and} \texttt{-\allowbreak{}prove-\allowbreak{}skip\ 1};
    the tool\textquotesingle s BMC mode applies both. \textbf{Strictness
    issues fixed (2026-09-13, branch \code{fix/q7n-slang-strict-rtl}).}
    The SoC now elaborates under yosys-slang in strict LRM mode --- no
    \code{--allow-use-before-declare}, no
    \texttt{-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}compat\ vcs} (4 errors → 0):
  \item
    \code{soc_top.sv} used \code{ext_irq}/\code{timer_irq}
    \textasciitilde190 lines before declaring them; the IRQ \code{logic}
    declarations now precede
    \code{u_ext_irq_sync}/\code{u_timer_irq_sync}. Ordering only.
  \item
    \code{pll_clkgen_pnr.sv} (ASAP7 \textbf{and} Sky130 copies) declared
    \texttt{parameter\ int\ unsigned\ PLL\_\allowbreak{}IMPL}, and
    \code{boot_rom.sv}\textquotesingle s \code{__pnr__} branch declared
    \texttt{parameter\ int\ unsigned\ MEM\_\allowbreak{}INIT\_\allowbreak{}FILE},
    while both are driven by \texttt{parameter\ string}. Synlig-era
    workarounds that only "worked" because sv2v emits untyped
    parameters; now \texttt{parameter\ string}. Neither shim reads the
    value. Re-verified: \code{soc_all} \textbf{183/183 PASS} (26 suite
    runs, including all four multiclock suites that exercise the IRQ
    synchronisers), Verilator lint clean, both sv2v netlists
    regenerated, and the equivalence tool now runs strict by default.
    \warn{} \textbf{Correction to the results above:} the
    \code{soc_top_sv2v.v} those numbers were measured against was
    \textbf{stale} --- it predated the \code{RAND_CDC_DELAY_*}
    parameters added to \code{cdc_2ff_sync.sv}. The full per-module
    re-sweep on the freshly regenerated netlist, in strict mode,
    reproduces them exactly: the same 25 modules proven (including
    \code{boot_rom} 79 and \code{pll_clkgen} 18, the two whose
    parameters changed), \code{sram_controller} proven at
    \code{MEM_WORDS=16}, and the same \code{pmu} 39 / \code{dma_engine}
    128 induction residue.
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{CPU macro LEF lacks AXI4 burst ports}
  (\code{awlen}/\code{wlast}/\ldots) --- abstract predates the M2 burst
  upgrade; SoC burst nets dangle at the macro boundary. Indicative-PPA
  acceptable; regenerate the abstract for a clean integration (bead
  \code{claude_verilog_test-g0o}).
\item
  Timing showed run-to-run variance (run 14 +113 ps vs run 15 −341 ps
  with the LEF change); the +113 ps margin is an indicative single-run
  result.
\end{itemize}

\end{record}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{PDN caveat (2026-09-12, beads \code{gyx} /
\code{4l8}) --- every ASAP7 run has an empty power
grid}{PDN caveat (2026-09-12, beads  / ) --- every ASAP7 run has an empty power grid}}\label{cr:run:phase5:pdn-caveat-2026-09-12-beads-gyx-4l8-every-asap7-run-has-an-empty-power-grid}

\begin{record}

Counting \code{dbSWire} shapes on the POWER/\allowbreak{}GROUND nets of
each run\textquotesingle s post-PDN ODB:

{\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-2\tabcolsep)*683/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-2\tabcolsep)*297/1000\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
ODB
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
PG shapes
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\code{pnr/sky130/soc/runs/RUN_2026-07-31_05-13-54/17-openroad-generatepdn}
& VPWR 65 610 / VGND 65 939 \\
\code{pnr/asap7/soc/runs/RUN_2026-08-09_14-36-16/17-openroad-generatepdn}
--- \textbf{run 23, the accepted \#96 sign-off} & \textbf{0 / 0} \\
\code{pnr/asap7/cpu/runs/RUN_2026-09-10_05-13-18/20-openroad-generatepdn}
--- 3.0.14 & \textbf{0 / 0} \\
\end{longtable}
}

}

The sky130 row is the positive control (same script, same OpenROAD 26Q2
binary), so this is a real absence, not a measurement artifact.

\textbf{Cause.} \code{pdngen} =
\texttt{check\_\allowbreak{}setup;\ build\_\allowbreak{}grids;\ write\_\allowbreak{}to\_\allowbreak{}db;\ reset\_\allowbreak{}shapes}.
On ASAP7,
\texttt{[ERROR\ PDN-\allowbreak{}0179]\ Unable\ to\ repair\ all\ channels}
fires \emph{inside} \code{build_grids}, before \code{write_to_db}. The
local warn-and-continue patch in \code{pdn.tcl} (bead \bead{ocm})
swallowed the error and let the flow continue --- with nothing written.
The flow\textquotesingle s "benign PDN-0179" line therefore meant "PDN
silently absent".

\textbf{What this withdraws.} The "PDN = benign tap-cell artifact" note
on the CPU/\allowbreak{}GPU/\allowbreak{}SoC sign-offs is withdrawn:
there were no violations because there were no shapes.
\code{analyze_power_grid}\textquotesingle s \code{PSM-0069} was never a
tap-cell connectivity problem either --- it had no grid to analyse.

\textbf{What it does \emph{not} change.} Timing, power and area were
always GRT/\allowbreak{}estimate-based and are unaffected in kind. What
does change is routing: every ASAP7 route ran with M1/M2/M5 free of PDN
metal, so all ASAP7 DRC counts (including the 171-DRC CPU result on bead
\bead{ocm}) are a lower bound, not closure. Re-run the routing gate once
the grid actually commits.

\textbf{Fix landed in the toolchain} (both LibreLane trees,
\code{librelane/scripts/openroad/pdn.tcl}): decompose \code{pdngen} so a
failed \code{build_grids} still commits what it built. On the CPU block
this goes 0 → 16 071 VDD / 17 151 VSS shapes. Residual: 550 VDD / 414
VSS unconnected shapes on M4 (the SRAM macro PG pins, skipped because
macro via insertion follows channel repair) + 18+18 on M1. Root-causing
PDN-0179 itself is tracked on bead \bead{4l8}.

\end{record}

\subsubsection{PDN fixed (2026-09-12, same day) --- ORFS-style grid
builds, and IR drop now runs}\label{cr:run:phase5:pdn-fixed-2026-09-12-same-day-orfs-style-grid-builds-and-ir-drop-now-runs}

\begin{record}

Root cause of the \code{PDN-0179} channel failure: the old grid
connected the \textbf{M1 followpin rails directly to the M5 straps} (a
four-layer stack) and used M2 only as a sparse 4.5 µm-pitch strap
running \emph{parallel} to those rails. ASAP7 std-cell rails sit on M1
running horizontally (M1\textquotesingle s non-preferred direction), so
most rails had nothing reachable above them and channel repair could not
fix it --- 27 unrepairable channels, all on M1, in the macro-free
regions x 82.19--124.96 µm and y 104.19--123.42 µm.

Fix ported from ORFS
\code{flow/platforms/asap7/openRoad/pdn/BLOCKS_grid_strategy.tcl}:
followpins on \textbf{both M1 and M2} (0.018 µm wide, 0.54 µm pitch ---
one per row), then M2→M5→M6, with the macro grid connecting M4↔M5
because the SRAM PG pins are on M4. Landed as
\src{pnr/asap7/pdn_asap7_orfs.tcl} (+ per-design \code{pdn_orfs.tcl}
wrappers, \code{PDN_CFG} in both \code{config_3014.json}).

Measured on the CPU block (\code{RUN_2026-09-10_05-13-18} step-18 ODB,
standalone harness):

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*345/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*366/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*269/1000\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
old grid
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
ORFS-style grid
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
remaining channels & 27 & \textbf{0} \\
PG shapes committed & \textbf{0} & 27 999 VDD / 27 213 VSS \\
\code{check_power_grid} & \code{PSM-0069} both nets & \textbf{PASS} both
nets \\
unconnected shapes / instances & 964 / 357 (partial-commit build) &
\textbf{0 / 0} \\
\end{longtable}
}

IR drop then needed one more thing: ASAP7 shipped \textbf{no via
resistances}, so PSM aborted with \code{PSM-0021} "Resistance map
contains invalid values" even with clean connectivity. Added
\code{VIAS_R} to both \code{config_3014.json} using
ORFS\textquotesingle s values (\code{flow/platforms/asap7/setRC.tcl}).
With that:

\begin{lstlisting}[language={}]
[INFO PSM-0040] All shapes on net VDD are connected.
Worstcase IR drop: 1.28e-03 V   Percentage drop: 0.18 %   (VDD)
Worstcase IR drop: 1.31e-03 V   Percentage drop: 0.19 %   (VSS)
\end{lstlisting}

\warn{} \textbf{Indicative only.} That number came from a standalone
harness on a \emph{floorplan-stage} ODB (step 18, before
placement/\allowbreak{}CTS/\allowbreak{}route) combined with the final
run\textquotesingle s SPEF, so instance power and shape geometry do not
correspond to the same design state. It demonstrates the flow works
end-to-end; it is \textbf{not} a sign-off IR figure. A full 3.0.14 CPU
run with \code{PDN_CFG} + \code{VIAS_R} + \code{RUN_IRDROP_REPORT=true}
is still required, and routing with a real grid on the tracks will be
harder than every previous ASAP7 route.

\end{record}

ORFS M5 2.16 / M6 4.32 & 50 / 8 & 12 GiB & 90 \% of iter 0 & 122 121 &
13.4 GB \\
ORFS M5 2.16 / M6 4.32 & 50 / 4 & 14 GiB & 90 \% of iter 0 & 122 121 &
13.4 GB \\
3× sparse 6.48 / 12.96 & 50 / 4 & 12 GiB & \textasciitilde75 \% of iter
0 & 83 207 & 11.2 GB \\
3× sparse 6.48 / 12.96 & 45 / 2 & 13 GiB & 80 \% of iter 0 & 112 764 &
12.8 GB \\
\end{longtable}
}

All four died inside \textbf{iteration 0 of 12}. For reference, the
PDN-free run peaked at 12.4 GB and did converge, to 1991 DRC --- so a
real grid costs roughly 20--30 \% more violations, and this host has no
headroom to absorb that.

\textbf{What did not work, and is worth not retrying blindly:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \emph{Sparser straps.} Three densities were measured (0.18 \%, 0.52
  \%, 1.37 \% worst-case IR). Violation counts barely moved --- the
  congestion is driven by the \textbf{M2 followpins}, one track per row,
  not by the M5/M6 mesh. Sparser straps buy IR margin, not routing
  headroom.
\item
  \emph{Lower utilization.} \code{FP_CORE_UTIL} 50→45 produced an
  identical violation count (44 103 at the same checkpoint) and
  \textbf{more} memory, because the larger die means more area to hold
  state for.
\item
  \emph{Fewer DRT threads.} 8→4→2 moved the peak only marginally; memory
  is dominated by violation markers, not per-worker state.
\item
  \emph{Avoiding M2 entirely.} Rails on M1 climbing straight to M3
  leaves every rail unconnected --- pdngen will not stack M1→M3 through
  M2. M1→M2 followpins are the only legal way up, so the M2 cost is
  structural.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Conclusion.} PDN-clean routing on this design needs a machine
with ≥32 GB, the same gate already recorded for the Sky130 GPU stages.
The PDN fix itself is verified independently of routing: the grid builds
and passes \code{check_power_grid} on CPU, GPU and SoC, and IR drop runs
end-to-end. What remains unverified is whether this design still closes
DRC once the grid occupies the tracks --- and the honest expectation,
given 1991 DRC PDN-free, is that it will need work.

\end{record}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{cpu\_clk CTS hold-skew fix + combined
rvb/0ah re-run (2026-09-17, beads
\code{0ah}/\code{rvb}/\code{ydw})}{cpu\_clk CTS hold-skew fix + combined rvb/0ah re-run (2026-09-17, beads //)}}\label{cr:run:phase5:cpu-clk-cts-hold-skew-fix-combined-rvb0ah-re-run-2026-09-17-beads-0ahrvbydw}

\begin{record}

Bead \code{0ah} (filed during \code{w3a}\textquotesingle s honest
post-GRT STA close-out) found the cpu\_clk clock-tree worst hold skew
regressing from -823.5 ps (post-CTS-repair, placement-estimate
parasitics) to -1687.9 ps (post-GRT, real in-session-GRT parasitics,
bead \bead{je8}\textquotesingle s methodology) at
\code{u_cpu_axi_cdc.u_w_fifo}. Diagnosis (live OpenROAD session, forced
\code{global_route} +
\texttt{estimate\_\allowbreak{}parasitics\ -\allowbreak{}global\_\allowbreak{}routing},
\code{report_parasitic_annotation}-gated): the regression was real, not
a placement-estimate artifact, but split into two effects --- a genuine
\textasciitilde79 ps CTS-level imbalance (\code{cpu_clk_i} and
\code{cpu_gated_clk_regs} are balanced as fully independent TritonCTS
trees across the \code{u_cpu_cg} ICG boundary, worsened by one
long-wire-repair buffer sized against placement-estimate parasitics),
and a separate, larger post-GRT-repair effect later found (by another
agent, bead \bead{0ah} comment) to be a mis-attribution ---
\code{w3a-sta-7}\textquotesingle s -1687.9 ps number was captured on
\code{RepairDesignPostGRT}\textquotesingle s output, \textbf{skipping}
\code{ResizerTimingPostGRT} (the hold-repair step), not a genuine
end-of-flow defect.

\textbf{Fix}: \code{CTS_BALANCE_LEVELS} + \code{CTS_OBSTRUCTION_AWARE}
(both existing, previously-unused LibreLane \code{Variable}s) in
\src{pnr/asap7/soc/config_multiclock_hier_0ah_cts.json}, validated
CTS-only
(\texttt{-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}from\ OpenROAD.\allowbreak{}CTS\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}to\ OpenROAD.\allowbreak{}STAMidPNR-\allowbreak{}1},
seeded from a pre-CTS checkpoint so synthesis/\allowbreak{}placement
stay identical to baseline): apples-to-apples at the same pipeline
stage, hold violators 705→0, hold WNS -639.3→0 ps, hold TNS -328 424→0
ps; setup violators 29 112→9 168, setup TNS 3.8× better. Re-validated
with real in-session-GRT parasitics (annotation-gated, 1548/204 196
unannotated, matching the unfixed baseline\textquotesingle s 1553/204
196 coverage): worst hold skew -902.1→+267.2 ps (cpu\_\allowbreak{}clk),
-187.1→+581.2 ps (sys\_clk) --- confirms the gain survives under real
parasitics, not just placement-estimate.

\end{record}

\subsubsection{CORRECTION (2026-09-17): the CTS fix above was a no-op;
reference numbers stand but are not reproducible from config}\label{cr:run:phase5:correction-2026-09-17-the-cts-fix-above-was-a-no-op-reference-numbers-stand-but-are-not-reproducible-from-config}

\begin{record}

The \code{CTS_BALANCE_LEVELS} / \code{CTS_OBSTRUCTION_AWARE} config keys
used in the section above are \textbf{not real LibreLane 2.4.13
variables} in this project\textquotesingle s runtime tree
(\texttt{\textasciitilde{}/\allowbreak{}Downloads/\allowbreak{}Github/\allowbreak{}librelane},
git commit \code{3562a8d}, 2026-02-27, unmodified). Both
\code{/nobackup/asap7_soc_runs/soc_rvb_0ah.log} and a later
\code{soc-ydw} run\textquotesingle s log print
\texttt{WARNING\ An\ unknown\ key\ 'CTS\_\allowbreak{}BALANCE\_\allowbreak{}LEVELS'\ /\allowbreak{}\ 'CTS\_\allowbreak{}OBSTRUCTION\_\allowbreak{}AWARE'\ /\allowbreak{}\ 'CTS\_\allowbreak{}CLK\_\allowbreak{}NET\_\allowbreak{}BUFFER'\ was\ provided},
and a direct read of \code{librelane/scripts/openroad/cts.tcl} confirms
it builds its \code{clock_tree_synthesis} argument list from only
\code{CTS_CLK_BUFFERS}, \code{CTS_ROOT_BUFFER},
\code{CTS_SINK_CLUSTERING_SIZE},
\code{CTS_SINK_CLUSTERING_MAX_DIAMETER},
\code{CTS_DISTANCE_BETWEEN_BUFFERS}, \code{CTS_DISABLE_POST_PROCESSING},
and \code{CTS_CLK_MAX_WIRE_LENGTH} --- never the two "fix" keys (nor the
pre-existing, also-unused \code{CTS_CLK_NET_BUFFER}, which predates bead
\bead{0ah}). \textbf{The config change in \code{78c5051}/\code{f80cb92}
was silently ignored by the tool; it did not cause the clean-hold
result.}

Re-investigated (read-only, no OpenROAD sessions or PD runs launched) by
diffing the reference run (\code{RUN_2026-09-16_19-19-45}) against a
same-config, RTL-updated run (\code{RUN_2026-09-17_06-25-54}, bead
\bead{ydw}\textquotesingle s +36 sys\_clk registered-read flops). Macro
placement is byte-identical in both (\code{u_cpu}/\code{u_gpu} FIXED at
the same DEF coordinates), ruling out macro movement.
\code{clk_i}\textquotesingle s sink count grew 45305→45340 (consistent
with the +36 flops) and its macro/\allowbreak{}register split
disappeared; but \code{cpu_clk_i}\textquotesingle s sink count is
\textbf{unchanged} (172 in both runs) and its split \emph{also}
disappeared --- ruling out "this net\textquotesingle s own sinks
changed" as the per-net trigger and pointing to a session-wide, not
per-net, internal mode selection. Direct evidence of two different
internal code paths: the reference run\textquotesingle s CTS log prints
"blockages from hard placement blockages and placed macros will be used"
(\code{CTS-0201}) six times and never prints \code{CTS-0200}; the
\code{ydw} run\textquotesingle s log prints a \emph{different} message
pair, \code{CTS-0200} ("0 placement blockages have been identified")
plus \code{CTS-0201} ("placed hard macros will be treated like
blockages"), four times --- the same message ID with two different
strings, on the identical bundled OpenROAD 26Q2 binary.

\textbf{Best-supported hypothesis (medium confidence,
log-evidence-based, not confirmed against TritonCTS source, which is
unavailable for this closed-source-packaged nix build; no bisection run
was performed)}: TritonCTS in this OpenROAD build auto-selects between
two internal macro/\allowbreak{}blockage handling paths based on an
undocumented, likely sink-count/\allowbreak{}density-driven threshold
evaluated once per \code{clock_tree_synthesis} invocation (not per net)
--- \code{clk_i}\textquotesingle s sink-count growth from bead
\bead{ydw}\textquotesingle s RTL crossed that threshold and changed
behaviour for \emph{all} clocks in that invocation, including the
unrelated \code{cpu_clk_i}. The tool itself has no seed or randomization
flag in its help text, so it is very likely deterministic given fixed
inputs; the observed run-to-run difference reflects a real
netlist/\allowbreak{}placement difference, not tool non-determinism.

\textbf{Real, deterministic fix (proposed, not applied --- requires
human approval to edit the shared \code{librelane} tree)}:
\code{openroad} help text confirms \code{clock_tree_synthesis} in this
26Q2 build genuinely supports a level-balancing toggle, an
obstruction-awareness toggle, and macro clustering
size/\allowbreak{}diameter settings. Patch \code{cts.tcl} to translate
four new env vars (\code{CTS_BALANCE_LEVELS},
\code{CTS_OBSTRUCTION_AWARE}, \code{CTS_MACRO_CLUSTERING_SIZE},
\code{CTS_MACRO_CLUSTERING_MAX_DIAMETER}) into the corresponding
\code{clock_tree_synthesis} arguments, and register matching
\code{Variable} declarations (mirroring the existing
\code{CTS_SINK_CLUSTERING_SIZE}/\code{CTS_SINK_CLUSTERING_MAX_DIAMETER}
pattern) so the config validator accepts them instead of silently
dropping them. Explicitly forcing obstruction-awareness plus macro
clustering size/\allowbreak{}diameter matched to the existing
sink-clustering values (8/10) would make the reference
run\textquotesingle s good behaviour reproducible by design rather than
incidental.

\textbf{Net effect}: the reference run\textquotesingle s numbers (hold
WNS 0 ps / 0 violators at \code{STAMidPNR-3}) are real measurements and
are \textbf{not retracted} --- but they are \textbf{not reproducible
from the config alone}; they depended on TritonCTS\textquotesingle s own
undocumented, netlist-sensitive behaviour on that specific run. Bead
\code{0ah} has been \textbf{reopened}. Beads \code{rvb} and \code{ydw}
and their findings in the section above are unaffected by this
correction.

\end{record}

\subsubsection{FURTHER CORRECTION (2026-09-17, second pass): the
"identical binary" premise above was also wrong}\label{cr:run:phase5:further-correction-2026-09-17-second-pass-the-identical-binary-premise-above-was-also-wrong}

\begin{record}

The correction section above assumed the reference run and
\code{soc-ydw} used the same OpenROAD 26Q2 binary and differed only in
RTL/\allowbreak{}netlist. That premise is wrong. Confirmed from
artifacts (read-only: log/\allowbreak{}config reads plus two cheap,
no-design \code{-version}/\code{help} queries on already- resident
binaries --- no new PD runs launched):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Attempt 4} (\code{RUN_2026-09-16_01-13-02}, the original bead
  \bead{0ah} baseline, -823.5 ps post-CTS) and \textbf{\code{w3a-sta-7}}
  (\code{RUN_2026-09-16_05-52-48}, the original -1687.9 ps "post-GRT"
  number): \src{memory/pd/run_state.md}\textquotesingle s own header for
  that campaign states "tool: LibreLane 2.4.13 ... bundled OpenROAD
  edf00dff / OpenSTA 2.6.0 (\code{OR_PATH_PREFIX=})" --- the
  \textbf{bundled} \code{edf00dff} build, not the pinned 26Q2 shim.
\item
  The \textbf{reference run} (\code{RUN_2026-09-16_19-19-45}, this
  bead\textquotesingle s closure basis): launched by a hand-rolled
  script that explicitly set \code{PATH} to the 26Q2 shim inside
  \code{nix-shell} --- confirmed \textbf{26Q2} by its
  1639-unannotated-driver count and a repair-progress table with
  Area/\allowbreak{}StTNS/\allowbreak{}EnTNS columns.
\item
  \textbf{\code{soc-ydw}} (\code{RUN_2026-09-17_06-25-54}): launched via
  \texttt{make\ librelane-\allowbreak{}asap7-\allowbreak{}soc-\allowbreak{}multiclock-\allowbreak{}hier},
  which never sets \code{OR_PATH_PREFIX} (new bead \bead{djm}) ---
  confirmed \textbf{\code{edf00dff}} by its 4663-unannotated-driver
  count, a repair-progress table lacking the
  Area/\allowbreak{}StTNS/\allowbreak{}EnTNS columns (single TNS column
  instead), and the live process binary itself reporting version
  \code{edf00dff99f6c40d67a30c0e22a8191c5d2ed9d6}.
\end{itemize}

So the real comparison this whole investigation made was \textbf{bundled
\code{edf00dff} (attempt 4, \code{w3a-sta-7}, \code{soc-ydw}) vs the
pinned 26Q2 (the one reference run)} --- a tool-version difference, not
a config or session-state difference. \code{clock_tree_synthesis} help
text differs between the two binaries: 26Q2 adds
\code{macro_clustering_size}/\code{macro_clustering_max_diameter} and a
\code{no_obstruction_aware} complement that \code{edf00dff} lacks
entirely. Since the shared \code{cts.tcl} never passes any
macro-specific flag explicitly, the two binaries\textquotesingle{} own
different built-in defaults for a clock net mixing macro and register
sinks is the simplest, best-supported explanation for the
macro/\allowbreak{}register split and latency-balancing difference ---
superseding the "session-wide TritonCTS mode" hypothesis in the
correction above (whose underlying log observations remain accurate,
just better explained this way).

\textbf{Revised conclusion}: bead \bead{0ah}\textquotesingle s apparent
"fix" was most likely the pinned 26Q2 build\textquotesingle s TritonCTS
macro-aware clustering, reached by accident because the validating
script bypassed a buggy Makefile target --- not any config key (already
confirmed a no-op) and not a documented, controllable CTS setting.

\textbf{New Makefile gap found (bead \code{claude_verilog_test-djm}, P1,
filed, not fixed)}: \code{librelane-asap7-soc-multiclock-hier} --- the
target this entire campaign
(\code{w3a}/\code{rvb}/\code{0ah}/\code{ydw}) has used --- is missing
from \src{pnr/Makefile}\textquotesingle s target-specific
\code{OR_PATH_PREFIX} assignment (\textasciitilde line 918), even though
\code{librelane-asap7-soc-multiclock} (without \code{-hier}) is present.
Its prerequisite \code{check-asap7-openroad} correctly verifies
\code{ASAP7_OPENROAD_BIN} points at a valid 26Q2 build and explicitly
rejects \code{edf00dff} --- but only checks the
\emph{variable\textquotesingle s value}, not that the
flow\textquotesingle s actual bare \code{openroad} invocation resolves
to it. The preflight passes while the flow silently uses the rejected
build anyway: precisely the "silent fallback to
LibreLane\textquotesingle s own OpenROAD" failure mode (bead \bead{xy6})
this check exists to prevent, via an untested code path. One-line fix
(add the target to the existing list) described in bead \bead{djm}; not
applied yet.

\textbf{Revised fix recommendation}: the \code{cts.tcl} passthrough
proposed in the correction above is most likely \textbf{unnecessary}. If
bead \bead{djm}\textquotesingle s Makefile fix is applied and the flow
consistently runs on the pinned 26Q2 build, 26Q2\textquotesingle s own
default TritonCTS macro-clustering behaviour may already reproduce the
clean-hold result with no new config knobs --- the passthrough should be
treated as a fallback only if a properly-pinned 26Q2 re-run does
\emph{not} reproduce it on its own.

Bead \code{0ah} remains open. The full \code{0ah}/\code{rvb}/\code{ydw}
comparison chain needs re-running on a consistently-pinned 26Q2 build
(after \code{djm}\textquotesingle s fix) before this bead can be
honestly closed.

\end{record}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{2026-09-17: bead \code{djm} fixed, clean
26Q2 re-run (\code{soc-ydw2}, \code{RUN_2026-09-17_09-01-42}) ---
\code{ydw} closed, \code{0ah} closed, \code{rvb} remains
open}{2026-09-17: bead  fixed, clean 26Q2 re-run (, ) ---  closed,  closed,  remains open}}\label{cr:run:phase5:2026-09-17-bead-djm-fixed-clean-26q2-re-run-soc-ydw2-run-2026-09-17-09-01-42-ydw-closed-0ah-closed-rvb-remains-open}

\begin{record}

\textbf{\code{djm} fix} (commit \code{eb7f4be}):
\src{pnr/Makefile}\textquotesingle s target-specific
\code{OR_PATH_PREFIX} list (\textasciitilde line 911-918) now includes
\code{librelane-asap7-soc-multiclock-hier}. \code{check-asap7-openroad}
(the shared preflight) was also hardened: it now resolves a bare
\code{openroad} inside \code{nix-shell} under the
\emph{caller\textquotesingle s} \code{OR_PATH_PREFIX} (propagated to
prerequisites by GNU Make) and fails the build if it does not land on
the pinned shim --- so a future target that forgets to join the list
fails loudly at preflight instead of silently mis-running on the bundled
build. An audit of every other ASAP7 target depending on
\code{check-asap7-openroad} found no further gaps.

\textbf{Re-run}:
\texttt{make\ librelane-\allowbreak{}asap7-\allowbreak{}soc-\allowbreak{}multiclock-\allowbreak{}hier\ SOC\_\allowbreak{}CONFIG=\allowbreak{}config\_\allowbreak{}multiclock\_\allowbreak{}hier\_\allowbreak{}rsz7\_\allowbreak{}0ah.\allowbreak{}json\ LIBRELANE\_\allowbreak{}EXTRA\_\allowbreak{}ARGS=\allowbreak{}"-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}to\ OpenROAD.\allowbreak{}STAMidPNR-\allowbreak{}3"},
unit \code{soc-ydw2}, run dir
\code{pnr/asap7/soc/runs/RUN_2026-09-17_09-01-42}, \code{EXIT_CODE=0}.
Binary confirmed live via \code{/proc/PID/exe} at the
Floorplan/\allowbreak{}PDN stage:
\code{/nix/store/hgqrwa4687mf7n0y2x6lcgx1kj42sfga-openroad-26Q2/bin/.openroad-wrapped}
--- the pinned 26Q2 build, launched through the \textbf{standard}
Makefile target for the first time in this campaign (not a hand-rolled

\textbf{STAMidPNR-3 final
(nom\_\allowbreak{}tt\_\allowbreak{}025C\_\allowbreak{}0p7V) vs.
reference \code{RUN_2026-09-16_19-19-45} (26Q2, rvb RTL, same config):}

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}

\textbf{Bead \code{ydw} --- CLOSED.} Violator-class analysis of
\code{violator_list.rpt} (33,206 lines):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The SRAM flat-array read-mux class (startpoint \code{u_sram} mem{[}{]}
  \code{QN} → endpoint \code{u_dma} or \code{u_bus.u_xbar};
  reference\textquotesingle s \#1 class, 9,259 violators, worst -1144.5
  ps) is \textbf{completely gone} --- 0 matches for
  \texttt{u\_\allowbreak{}sram.\allowbreak{}*→u\_\allowbreak{}dma} or
  \texttt{u\_\allowbreak{}sram.\allowbreak{}*→u\_\allowbreak{}bus.\allowbreak{}u\_\allowbreak{}xbar}
  in the final violator list.
\item
  It is replaced by a much smaller, module-internal class,
  \texttt{u\_\allowbreak{}sram\ →\ u\_\allowbreak{}sram} (the mem{[}{]}→
  \code{r_data_q} head-register segment ydw\textquotesingle s design
  intentionally confines the mux\textquotesingle s fan-out to):
  \textbf{32 violators} (matches the 32-bit read-data width),
  \textbf{worst slack -947.29 ps}. This is worse than the
  pre-measurement estimate of \textasciitilde-300 ps (about 3x), a real
  and substantial residual, but it no longer propagates outside
  \code{sram_controller.sv} and is no longer the
  design\textquotesingle s critical path. Post-synthesis instance names
  are fully anonymized (the flat 4096×32 array is fully
  deduped/\allowbreak{}optimized by yosys), so the exact bit/word cannot
  be cited by RTL name.
\item
  The new \#1 class overall (by count and by worst slack) is
  \textbf{not} a read-side effect at all:
  \texttt{u\_\allowbreak{}gpu/\allowbreak{}m\_\allowbreak{}axi\_\allowbreak{}wvalid\ →\ u\_\allowbreak{}sram}
  (rvb\textquotesingle s Path A write-decode residual), 16,400
  violators, worst -1050.16 ps = the run\textquotesingle s new overall
  setup WNS. \texttt{u\_\allowbreak{}dma\ →\ u\_\allowbreak{}sram}
  (DMA\textquotesingle s own analog of the same pattern) is a close
  second: 14,392 violators, worst -975.65 ps. Together these two classes
  are 92.7\% of all setup violators.
\item
  A 2-stage mux split (e.g. 32:1×32:1 instead of flat 1024:1) for the
  residual mem{[}{]}→\code{r_data_q} path was assessed as a plausible
  future improvement given the -947 ps residual, but judged \textbf{not
  urgent}: the write-path classes are \textasciitilde100 ps worse and
  \textgreater500x more numerous, making them the higher-leverage target
  for the next PD iteration. Not implemented.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Bead \code{rvb} --- remains OPEN.} Path A
(\code{u_gpu/m_axi_wvalid} → flat write decode) is not gone; it is now
the design\textquotesingle s \#1 class (16,400 violators / -1050.16 ps
vs. the reference measurement\textquotesingle s 6,093 / -954.8 ps). A
DMA-side analog of the same pattern
(\texttt{u\_\allowbreak{}dma\ →\ u\_\allowbreak{}sram}, 14,392 / -975.65
ps) also appears. Whether Path A\textquotesingle s own severity truly
regressed, or the count/\allowbreak{}attention simply redistributed onto
it once the read-mux class (ydw) was removed, is not isolated by this
measurement --- recorded on the bead as an open question. Recommended as
the next PD target given it is now \textgreater90\% of all setup
violators combined with its DMA analog.

\textbf{Bead \code{0ah} --- CLOSED}, resolved by pinning 26Q2
(\code{djm}). Hold is clean end-to-end and clean \emph{before} dedicated
hold repair even runs (0 violators already at the post-CTS checkpoint,
\code{32-openroad-stamidpnr-1}, WS +36.64 ps). Both
clocks\textquotesingle{} skew is healthy positive and within
\textasciitilde13-16 ps of the original (accidental) reference-run
measurement, now reproduced through the \textbf{standard} Makefile path.
This confirms the bead\textquotesingle s own final root-cause conclusion
--- 26Q2\textquotesingle s TritonCTS macro-aware clustering default, not
the confirmed-no-op \code{CTS_BALANCE_LEVELS}/
\code{CTS_OBSTRUCTION_AWARE} config keys --- and satisfies the
bead\textquotesingle s own stated reopen condition (a clean re-run of
the full \code{0ah}/\code{rvb}/\code{ydw} chain on a consistently-pinned
26Q2 build via the fixed Makefile target). No \code{cts.tcl} passthrough
patch is needed.

Note: this run stopped at
\texttt{-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}to\ OpenROAD.\allowbreak{}STAMidPNR-\allowbreak{}3}
(no detailed routing / DRC / antenna / LVS steps ran), so it is a
timing-only measurement, not a routing sign-off; the power figures above
include the \code{Macro} power group (192.5 mW), unlike some earlier
ASAP7 sign-offs in this document that reported fabric-only figures as
the headline number.

\end{record}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{2026-09-18: 2-stage group-distributed
write pipeline (\code{soc-rvb2}, \code{RUN_2026-09-17_20-41-11}) ---
\code{rvb}
CLOSED}{2026-09-18: 2-stage group-distributed write pipeline (, ) ---  CLOSED}}\label{cr:run:phase5:2026-09-18-2-stage-group-distributed-write-pipeline-soc-rvb2-run-2026-09-17-20-41-11-rvb-closed}

\begin{record}

\textbf{RTL} (commit \code{23bacae}, \code{feat/rvb-sram-write-path}):
replaces the prior rvb iteration\textquotesingle s
\code{word_sel_q}-gated per-word \code{always_ff} (still a same-cycle
combinational function of \code{s_wvalid} via \code{w_commit}, gating a
\code{MEM_WORDS}-wide AND array off the address-phase one-hot decode ---
the measured \#1 violator class on \code{RUN_2026-09-17_09-01-42},
16,400 / -1050.16 ps) with a 3-stage pipeline in
\src{rtl/soc/sram_controller.sv}
(\texttt{ifndef\ SRAM\_\allowbreak{}SKY130} only): stage 1 registers the
accepted W beat
(\code{wr_valid1_q}/\code{wdata1_q}/\code{wstrb1_q}/\code{idx1_q});
stage 2 splits \code{MEM_WORDS} (1024 for this SoC config) into
\code{NGROUPS}=32 groups of \code{GROUP_WORDS}=32 words, each with its
own \code{grp_we_q}/\code{grp_wdata_q} registers (\code{grp_wdata_q}
explicitly self-holds when its group is not selected, which keeps Yosys
\code{opt_merge} from collapsing the 32 replicas back into one
high-fanout register --- verified via a standalone
\texttt{opt\_\allowbreak{}merge\ -\allowbreak{}share\_\allowbreak{}all}
pass, 0 cells removed); stage 3 writes \code{mem[]} per-group, gated
only by that group\textquotesingle s own registered enable. A word lands
in \code{mem[]} 2 cycles after its W handshake; \code{WREADY} stays
unconditional (1 beat/\allowbreak{}cycle sustained); \code{BVALID} is
held off via a new \code{W_DRAIN} FSM state until the
burst\textquotesingle s last beat has actually landed. Verified:
\code{lint_soc}/\code{lint_soc_sky130} 0 errors, \code{asap7-soc-sv2v}
regenerates cleanly (6821→6921 lines), \src{tb/cocotb/soc}
\code{sram_controller} 15/15 on both \code{SRAM_TARGET=freepdk45} and
\code{SRAM_TARGET=sky130}, plus \code{soc_all} 196/196 /
\code{phase2_all} 112/112 / \code{soc_stress} PASS (commit
\code{693deb5}).

\textbf{Re-run}: same target/\allowbreak{}config/\code{--to} point as
the \code{ydw}/\code{0ah} campaign ---

unit \code{soc-rvb2}, run dir
\code{pnr/asap7/soc/runs/RUN_2026-09-17_20-41-11}, \code{EXIT_CODE=0}.

(\texttt{ok:\allowbreak{}\ 'openroad'\ resolves\ to\ pinned\ shim});
binary use was reconfirmed live by the coordinator via

\textbf{Annotation check}: all 3,156 unannotated drivers classify into
exactly two non-signal categories, both unchanged in composition from
the reference run --- \code{clkload<N>/Y} dummy clock-balancing load
cells (3,073, up from the reference\textquotesingle s 1,343 --- CTS
inserted more of them to balance the larger/\allowbreak{}redistributed
clock tree from the \textasciitilde4,155 net new sequential cells) and
83 \code{u_cpu} macro boundary pins (\code{debug_rs2_data_o[*]},
\code{debug_state_o[*]}, \code{trap_cause_o[*]}, \code{trap_taken_o},
\code{axi_arsize_o[*]}, \code{axi_awsize_o[*]} ---
unconnected/\allowbreak{}tied debug and fixed-burst-size outputs on the
CPU macro, \textbf{identical set, identical count} in both runs). Zero
partially-unannotated drivers in either run. No real internal signal
driver is unannotated in this run; the 2.2x increase is fully explained
by the larger clock tree, not a parasitic-annotation quality regression
on the data path.
