\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{(a) Write classes} --- this bead\textquotesingle s closure
  criterion.
  \texttt{u\_\allowbreak{}gpu/\allowbreak{}m\_\allowbreak{}axi\_\allowbreak{}wvalid\ →\ u\_\allowbreak{}sram}
  (Path A, the reference\textquotesingle s \#1 class: 16,400 violators,
  worst -1050.16 ps) has \textbf{0 matches} in this run --- completely
  gone. \texttt{u\_\allowbreak{}dma\ →\ u\_\allowbreak{}sram} (Path
  A\textquotesingle s DMA analog, reference\textquotesingle s \#2:
  14,392 violators, worst -975.65 ps) is reduced to \textbf{32
  violators, worst -349.60 ps} --- a 99.8\% count reduction and a 64\%
  slack improvement, and no longer close to the design\textquotesingle s
  critical path. Both original Path A classes are resolved by the
  closure criterion\textquotesingle s own terms: neither is limiting.
\item
  \textbf{(b) Worst remaining class} ---
  \texttt{u\_\allowbreak{}sram\ →\ u\_\allowbreak{}sram}, 32 violators,
  worst -727.37 ps (= the design\textquotesingle s overall setup WNS).
  Traced via \code{final/nl/soc_top.nl.v} (fully anonymized cell/net
  names post-synthesis, so RTL attribution required structural
  fingerprinting rather than name matching): this class has \textbf{8
  unique startpoints fanning out to 32 unique endpoints} (e.g. one
  startpoint instance drives 4 different endpoints). This
  many-drivers-to-few / few-drivers- to-many cardinality is the opposite
  of \code{ydw}\textquotesingle s read-mux structure (\code{mem[r_idx]},
  up to 1024 candidate mem-word startpoints converging 1:1 onto each of
  the 32 \code{r_data_q} bit endpoints --- which would show 32
  \emph{unique} startpoints each with exactly \emph{one} endpoint, not 8
  startpoints fanning to 32 endpoints). It matches instead the
  \textbf{new rvb stage-2→stage-3 write-commit fanout}:
  \code{grp_wdata_q[gk]} (32 bits, shared per group) combinationally
  fans out, through the \code{grp_we_q[gk][gw]}-gated mux, to up to
  \code{GROUP_WORDS}=32 different \code{mem[]} word endpoints within its
  own group --- exactly the endpoint count observed. This is
  rvb\textquotesingle s \textbf{own} fix\textquotesingle s residual, not
  a resurgence of \code{ydw}\textquotesingle s read segment: the
  by-design bound on this fanout (≤32 endpoints per group, vs. the
  pre-fix design\textquotesingle s ≤1024-wide single-cycle AND array) is
  working as intended, but is not yet zero. \code{ydw}\textquotesingle s
  read-mux class (\code{u_sram}→\code{u_sram} with the reverse
  cardinality signature) does \textbf{not} appear anywhere in this
  violator list --- 0 matches --- so \code{ydw}\textquotesingle s fix
  continues to hold with no reopening needed at this design point.
\item
  \textbf{(c) \code{u_pll_sub} classes} --- new in this run, absent from
  the reference run\textquotesingle s classes entirely (reference has
  only a small, differently-named
  \texttt{u\_\allowbreak{}apb\_\allowbreak{}pll2\_\allowbreak{}cdc\ →\ u\_\allowbreak{}cpu\_\allowbreak{}pll\_\allowbreak{}sub}
  class, 8 violators / -104.00 ps at STAMidPNR-3). In this run,
  \texttt{u\_\allowbreak{}pll\_\allowbreak{}sub\ →\ u\_\allowbreak{}dma}
  (18 violators, -140.39 ps) and
  \texttt{u\_\allowbreak{}pll\_\allowbreak{}sub\ →\ u\_\allowbreak{}sram}
  (11 violators, -111.48 ps) both appear at \code{STAMidPNR-1}
  (post-CTS, pre-repair). The post-CTS repair pass (\code{STAMidPNR-2})
  eliminates both. Post-GRT,
  \texttt{u\_\allowbreak{}pll\_\allowbreak{}sub\ →\ u\_\allowbreak{}dma}
  stays gone, but
  \texttt{u\_\allowbreak{}pll\_\allowbreak{}sub\ →\ u\_\allowbreak{}sram}
  reappears at 15 violators / -76.01 ps by \code{STAMidPNR-3} (new
  post-route parasitics, then partially re-repaired). \code{u_pll_sub}
  has no functional connection to \code{sram_controller.sv} in this
  commit --- the most likely explanation is an incidental
  floorplan/\allowbreak{}CTS side effect of adding \textasciitilde5,168
  raw RTL register bits to \code{u_sram} (see cost accounting below),
  which shifted placement density/\allowbreak{}clock-tree balancing
  enough to put a pre-existing, previously-comfortable PLL-subsystem
  fabric path under new (small, largely-repaired) pressure. Not
  investigated further this session; worth a watch-item if it grows in a
  future re-run, but it is 32-62x smaller than the
  design\textquotesingle s dominant classes at every checkpoint and does
  not change the overall conclusion.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Cost accounting}: the fix adds 5,168 raw RTL register bits
(\code{NGROUPS}×(\code{GROUP_WORDS}×\code{SW} \code{grp_we_q} bits +
\code{DW} \code{grp_wdata_q} bits) = 32×(32×4 + 32) = 5,120, plus 48
stage-1/\allowbreak{}drain bits), replacing the prior
iteration\textquotesingle s 1,024-bit \code{word_sel_q} one-hot register
(net raw RTL delta ≈ +4,144 bits) --- consistent with the measured net
placed \code{sequential_cell} count increase of +4,155 (46,297→50,452).
Despite this, \textbf{placed area and utilization are essentially flat}
(166,960→166,811 µm², 66.86\%→66.80\%): the added flip-flop area is
offset by 13,181 \emph{fewer} timing-repair buffers needed overall
(64,005→50,824 \code{timing_repair_buffer} instances, since far less
setup repair work remains), and \textbf{total power went down}, not up
(293.2→283.6 mW, -3.3\%; fabric-only 100.7→89.6 mW, -11.0\%) ---
switching power fell 24.1\% (42.7→32.4 mW) from the same
reduced-buffer-churn effect, outweighing the small increase in
sequential internal/\allowbreak{}leakage power.
\code{RepairDesignPostGPL} (pre-route legalization/\allowbreak{}repair,
stage 28) itself got \textbf{slower}, not faster, despite inserting
fewer buffers at that stage (48,047 vs. reference\textquotesingle s
61,720 \code{timing_repair_buffer} instances added there): 1h30m42s vs.
the reference\textquotesingle s 35m45s (2.5x) --- attributed to
per-iteration legalization/\allowbreak{}congestion-analysis overhead
scaling with the \textasciitilde19,112 additional design instances at a
pre-route stage, not to more repair work. Total flow wall time to
\code{STAMidPNR-3} was nonetheless comparable end-to-end
(\textasciitilde4h25m vs. \textasciitilde4h18m for the reference), since
the much smaller post-GRT violator count more than paid back the
\code{RepairDesignPostGPL} slowdown in \code{ResizerTimingPostGRT}.

\textbf{Bead \code{rvb} --- CLOSED.} Both Path A write classes this bead
was filed against (\texttt{u\_\allowbreak{}gpu\ →\ u\_\allowbreak{}sram}
and its DMA analog
\texttt{u\_\allowbreak{}dma\ →\ u\_\allowbreak{}sram}) are no longer
limiting per the criteria above --- one is fully eliminated, the other
reduced by two orders of magnitude in count and 64\% in worst slack. The
closure is honest, not a claim of zero residual: a new, smaller,
\emph{by-design bounded} write-commit fanout residual
(\texttt{u\_\allowbreak{}sram\ →\ u\_\allowbreak{}sram}, 32 violators,
-727.37 ps, rvb\textquotesingle s own stage-2→stage-3 group fanout) is
now the design\textquotesingle s worst class overall, and
\texttt{u\_\allowbreak{}cpu\ →\ u\_\allowbreak{}cpu\_\allowbreak{}axi\_\allowbreak{}cdc}
(504 violators, -190.37 ps, a CPU-domain-internal CDC-bridge path
unrelated to either Path A or the SRAM controller) is now the largest
class \emph{by count}. Both are recorded as follow-up candidates, not
reopened against this bead, since neither is the GPU/DMA write-fanout
pattern this bead was filed to track. \code{ydw}\textquotesingle s
read-mux class does not resurface in this run (0 matches) and needs no
action.

Note: like the \code{ydw}/\code{0ah} measurements above, this run
stopped at
\texttt{-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}to\ OpenROAD.\allowbreak{}STAMidPNR-\allowbreak{}3}
(no detailed routing / DRC / antenna / LVS), so it is a timing-only
measurement, not a routing sign-off, and the power figures include the
\code{Macro} group unless stated as "fabric-only" above.

\end{record}

(+12.6\%) \\

\end{longtable}
}

}

\code{CTS_SINK_CLUSTERING_SIZE}=8 and
\code{CTS_SINK_CLUSTERING_MAX_DIAMETER}=10 µm (both already tightened
from LibreLane defaults 25/50 µm by the Run-19 \code{cpu_clk} fix
recorded earlier in this file) are identical config in both runs, and
clock-root count (6) and tree depth are unchanged. The entire skew story
is a genuinely larger, more heavily-loaded \code{sys_clk} sink
population (an exact +4,155-sink match to the RTL delta) needing 2.3×
more dummy-load balancing and 6 more explicit delay-balancing buffers to
stay latency-matched --- not a deeper or differently-shaped tree.

\textbf{Where the skew lives.}
\code{report_clock_skew}\textquotesingle s worst-case pair is the
\emph{same role} in both runs:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Source (latest arrival):
  \texttt{u\_\allowbreak{}pmu.\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}20\{7,\allowbreak{}8\}\_\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}CLK}
  --- a PMU reset-sync flop. \code{pmu.sv} has been untouched since
  commit \code{17b18ec}, long before rvb\textquotesingle s
  \code{23bacae} (which only touches \code{sram_controller.sv}). Latency
  820.97 → 1018.87 ps setup (+197.9 ps), 794.71 → 984.67 ps hold
  (+189.96 ps).
\item
  Target (earliest arrival): \code{u_gpu_cg.u_icg/CLK} --- the GPU
  clock-gate cell. Latency −140.43 → −151.47 ps setup (+11.0 ps
  magnitude), −144.29 → −155.70 ps hold (+11.4 ps).
\end{itemize}

\textasciitilde95\% of the skew growth is the \emph{source} side getting
later (197.9 of 186.9 ps setup net delta, partly offset by the
target\textquotesingle s own 11.0 ps move), not the write-pipeline
registers themselves. Neither \code{u_pmu} nor \code{u_gpu_cg} is part
of the rvb write pipeline (\code{grp_we_q}/\code{grp_wdata_q} live in
\code{u_sram}). Cross-referenced against
\code{38-openroad-stamidpnr-3/violator_list.rpt} (781 setup entries in
rvb2): \textbf{0 matches for \code{u_pmu} or \code{u_gpu_cg}}. Every
violator is a \code{u_sram}-rooted class already tracked against bead
\bead{rvb} separately (worst
\texttt{u\_\allowbreak{}sram\ →\ u\_\allowbreak{}sram}, −727.37 ps).
Conclusion: \code{report_clock_skew} reports a global (max sink latency
− min sink latency) bound across the whole clock net, not a real
launch/\allowbreak{}capture timing arc. \code{u_pmu}\textquotesingle s
branch is wherever TritonCTS\textquotesingle s own latency-balancing
step (\code{CTS-0033}/\code{CTS-0036}) pads delay to match the
tree\textquotesingle s new, higher max latency, driven by needing to
buffer +4,155 real sinks and 2.3× more dummy loads elsewhere in the same
6-root, unchanged-depth tree; \code{u_gpu_cg.u_icg} is a small, shallow
subtree mostly untouched by that rebalancing, so it anchors the "early"
end and the gap widens.

\textbf{Risk verdict:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{(a) Insertion-delay growth} --- real, and it is the whole
  story: same tree depth (12--13), same 6 roots, +12.6\% final sinks,
  +133\% dummy-load cells. Benign by construction --- TritonCTS padding
  fast branches up to match a genuinely slower one, not failing to
  balance.
\item
  \textbf{(b) Real-path impact} --- none found. 0/781
  \code{violator_list.rpt} matches for either skew-report instance.
  Overall setup timing improved sharply in the same run despite the skew
  growth (WNS −1050.16 → −727.37 ps, TNS −14,423,700 → −60,665 ps,
  violators 33,196 → 781) --- the skew metric and real critical-path
  health are decoupled here.
\item
  \textbf{(c) Hold margin} --- thinned but stayed clean: WS 38.01 →
  31.26 ps (−6.75 ps, −17.8\%), WNS/TNS still 0/0 in both runs. The
  worst hold path changed \textbf{domain}, not just magnitude:
  ref\textquotesingle s worst hold path is on \code{sys_clk}
  (\code{u_irq_ctrl._145_} → \code{u_irq_ctrl._140_});
  rvb2\textquotesingle s is on \code{cpu_clk}
  (\code{u_cpu_axi_cdc.u_b_fifo.u_wr_ptr_to_rd._18_} → \code{._21_}, a
  pre-existing async-FIFO pointer CDC path).
  \code{cpu_clk}\textquotesingle s own skew stayed flat (285/236 →
  281/230 ps) across the two runs, so this hold thinning is \textbf{not}
  downstream of the \code{sys_clk} skew growth investigated here --- it
  is a separate, minor, \code{cpu_clk}-side CTS-rebalancing side effect.
  Worth watching in future runs (\code{cpu_clk} hold WS, 31.26 ps, is
  now the thinner of the two margins, and its worst path is a CDC
  pointer-sync chain --- exactly the path class most sensitive to hold
  erosion), but out of scope for this investigation.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Mitigation considered, not applied.} LibreLane
2.4.13\textquotesingle s \code{OpenROAD.CTS} step
(\code{librelane/scripts/openroad/cts.tcl}, cross-checked against the
\code{config_vars} list in \code{librelane/librelane/steps/openroad.py})
exposes only \emph{global} (all-clock) knobs:
\code{CTS_SINK_CLUSTERING_SIZE} (default 25, already 8 here),
\code{CTS_SINK_CLUSTERING_MAX_DIAMETER} (default 50 µm, already 10 µm
here), \code{CTS_DISTANCE_BETWEEN_BUFFERS} (default 0/off),
\code{CTS_CLK_MAX_WIRE_LENGTH} (default 0/off),
\code{CTS_MAX_CAP}/\code{CTS_MAX_SLEW} (unset). Re-confirmed (consistent
with bead \bead{0ah}\textquotesingle s own finding) that
\code{CTS_BALANCE_LEVELS} and \code{CTS_OBSTRUCTION_AWARE} --- present
as historical comments in \code{config_multiclock_hier_rsz7_0ah.json}
--- are not read by \code{cts.tcl} and remain no-ops; this investigation
found no evidence to revise that conclusion. There is \textbf{no
per-clock CTS override} in this LibreLane version (the config
file\textquotesingle s own \code{AH_0AH_COMMENT_4} note already flags
this). A candidate mitigation --- tighten
\code{CTS_SINK_CLUSTERING_MAX_DIAMETER} further (10 µm →
\textasciitilde5 µm) to force finer, more uniform sink clustering and
reduce the dummy-load/\allowbreak{}delay-buffer disparity --- is
credible but \textbf{global}: it would also perturb
\code{cpu_clk}\textquotesingle s tree, which per (c) above now carries
the thinner hold margin (31.26 ps). Validation cost: a full
\code{CTS}-through-\code{STAMidPNR-3} re-run (\textasciitilde4--4.5 h,
matching the two runs measured here) checking \code{sys_clk} setup skew,
\code{cpu_clk} hold WS (must stay positive), and overall setup WNS/TNS
(must not regress from −727.37 ps / −60,665 ps). \textbf{Not run this
session} --- read-only investigation, no PD run permitted while another
agent\textquotesingle s Verilator regression is in flight.

\textbf{Bead \code{ea0} --- CLOSED.} Explained and benign, monitored:
the skew growth is a real, exactly RTL-delta-matched consequence of
TritonCTS balancing a genuinely larger \code{sys_clk} sink population,
it does not touch any real violating timing path, and it does not
explain the small, separately-rooted \code{cpu_clk}-domain hold-margin
thinning noted in (c) above (recorded here as a forward watch-item, not
reopened as a new bead).

\end{record}

\begin{quote}
\warn{} \textbf{Every "DRC / antenna / errors: 0 / 0 / 0" claim in this
document is unsupported.} Confirmed 2026-08-16 (bead
\code{claude_verilog_test-xy6}): \code{OpenROAD.DetailedRouting} on this
repo\textquotesingle s ASAP7 flow errors out during pin access
(\texttt{[ERROR\ DRT-\allowbreak{}0074]\ No\ access\ point\ for\ PIN/\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}})
before any wire is routed, and a local LibreLane patch
(\code{drt.tcl}\textquotesingle s \texttt{catch\ \{\}} around
\code{detailed_route}) lets the flow continue anyway --- so every
downstream DRC/ antenna/\allowbreak{}slew/\allowbreak{}cap checker
reports 0 because nothing was routed to violate, not because routing is
clean. Verified directly on the 8-run \#96 \code{pin_order.cfg} tuning
campaign (2026-08-08/\allowbreak{}09, 401 \code{DRT-0074} errors and
zero \code{ROUTED} net records in every run\textquotesingle s final
DEF); the artifacts for Run 43 and the M7 re-sign-off no longer exist on
disk to re-check directly, but the catch patch was already in place
during this campaign (see the Run 6/7 note in
\src{memory/pd/knowledge.md}, predating Run 43 by weeks), so there is no
basis to assume they are exempt. \textbf{What survives:} the
setup/\allowbreak{}hold
WNS/\allowbreak{}TNS/\allowbreak{}fmax/\allowbreak{}power/\allowbreak{}area
numbers below are computed from a real placed netlist and are
self-consistent on their own terms (pre-route GRT- estimated parasitics,
same as always) --- only the
DRC/\allowbreak{}antenna/\allowbreak{}routing-clean claims are
withdrawn. Full mechanism, the cross-block routed/\allowbreak{}unrouted
table, and the fail-loud gate that now catches this:
\cref{cr:run:phase5} § "Routing / physical-closure caveat".
\end{quote}

\begin{quote}
\warn{} **Every setup/\allowbreak{}hold
WNS/\allowbreak{}TNS/\allowbreak{}violator-count figure in this document
that was read off LibreLane 2.4.13\textquotesingle s final
\code{metrics.json} (i.e. every run below that never reached a real
\code{OpenROAD.STAPostPNR} with an extracted SPEF) is
\code{OpenROAD.STAMidPNR-3} output --- the stock post-GRT STA step. Bead
\code{claude_verilog_test-8f3} (found/\allowbreak{}fixed 2026-09-16)
proved that step\textquotesingle s \code{sta/corner.tcl} calls
\texttt{estimate\_\allowbreak{}parasitics\ -\allowbreak{}global\_\allowbreak{}routing}
on a loaded ODB \textbf{without an in-session \code{global_route}}, and
OpenROAD does not recover GRT parasitics from a saved ODB --- so the
reported timing runs on \textbf{zero-wire / unannotated} parasitics
(measured 94.44\% of drivers unannotated on a fresh CPU-block run of the
\emph{exact same config family} as the Run 43 / M7 rows below, falsely
reporting 0/0/0 setup and hold violations). The fix
(\code{STA_POSTGRT_INSESSION_GRT=1}, opt-in, now applied to the shared
LibreLane tree) re-routes in-session before the STA read; every WNS/TNS
number below predates that fix and was never re-measured with it. This
is \textbf{separate} from the \code{xy6} routing caveat above ---
\code{xy6} is about detailed routing committing no wires (a DRT
pin-access failure); \code{8f3} is about the STA step itself not
re-deriving parasitics from whatever GRT guides \emph{do} exist.
Re-validation status, honest current-RTL replacement figures, and
per-run classification: bead \code{claude_verilog_test-0p6}, inline
notes below, and \cref{cr:run:phase5} § "Power figure
caveat" (bead \bead{ew3}) for the SoC side.
\end{quote}

\subsubsection{Why 1418 MHz no longer holds, and what was tried}\label{cr:run:asap7:why-1418-mhz-no-longer-holds-and-what-was-tried}

\begin{record}

At the Run 43 target of 705 ps the flat-case M7 RTL misses setup by WNS
−56 ps (run \code{RUN_2026-06-02_17-49-55}). \textbf{True limiter} (PD
diagnosis, two-run compare): an \textbf{836-fanout counter-write-enable
broadcast register} --- synthesis collapses the
\texttt{(csr\_\allowbreak{}access\ \&\&\ !csr\_\allowbreak{}illegal)}
counter write-select into one node feeding all \textasciitilde224 M7
counter flops (pre-PNR slew \textasciitilde3128 ps). The 64-bit
mcycle/\allowbreak{}minstret increment adders sit behind it.

A CSR read-mux two-level restructure (commit \code{9d8f64a}) was
attempted and \textbf{reverted} (commit \code{b0b143e}): functionally
clean (192/192) but timing-ineffective --- worst-path depth unchanged
(56→58 gates, synthesis re-flattens), and its intermediate range signals
degraded placement, dropping the post-GRT resizer gain from 111 ps to 10
ps → WNS −89 ps / TNS −7646 ps at 705 ps (run
\code{RUN_2026-06-02_18-52-22}).

\textbf{Disposition}: rather than register the counter increment-enables
(which would add 1-cycle counter latency + minstret-exactness edge cases
for a vanity frequency the SoC does not need), the clock was relaxed to
780 ps for a clean WNS≥0 standalone sign-off. The 836-fanout
counter-enable restructure remains an available future RTL optimization
if a \textgreater1300 MHz standalone CPU is ever required. Bead
\code{3ux} closed at 1282 MHz.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\end{record}

not \textasciitilde500 MHz. \\
\textbf{Era 2: Units corrected} & 32--41 & Values in
\textbf{picoseconds} (\texttt{-\allowbreak{}period\ 720} = 720 ps = 1389
MHz target) & Real STA. Frequency campaigns meaningful. \\
\end{longtable}
}

}

The fix landed at Run 33: SDC rewritten with comment
\texttt{\#\ IMPORTANT:\allowbreak{}\ All\ time\ values\ are\ in\ PICOSECONDS}.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\end{record}

\begin{record}

\textbf{WNS: −1147 ps \textbar{} fmax est. \textasciitilde466 MHz}

First complete ASAP7 LibreLane flow run. Establishes baseline PPA after
all infrastructure patches (RSZ-0089 set\_\allowbreak{}rc.tcl, DRT-0074
catch block, PDN non-fatal exit). 1.0 ns SDC target (bug: resolved to
1.0 ps).

\begin{itemize}

  (PDN-0179)

\subsubsection{Run 11 (May 8, 2026)}\label{cr:run:asap7:run-11-may-8-2026}

\begin{record}

\textbf{WNS: −844.7 ps → +214 ps vs Run 9 \textbar{} Power: 31.81 mW
\textbar{} fmax est. \textasciitilde542 MHz}

\textbf{Critical discovery}: All 4,102+ setup violations trace to a
single FF \code{_54587_} --- the I-cache FSM state register, a
\code{DFFASRHQNx1} async-reset cell. The QN (inverted Q) output adds
extra inversion delay.

reveals the next.

\begin{record}

\textbf{Milestone}: Discovered that
\texttt{create\_\allowbreak{}clock\ -\allowbreak{}period\ 1.\allowbreak{}9}
resolved to \textbf{1.9 ps} against ASAP7 Liberty\textquotesingle s
\texttt{time\_\allowbreak{}unit:\allowbreak{}\ 1ps}. All prior WNS
values represented path delays relative to \textasciitilde2 ps required
time. True design fmax was \textasciitilde1147 MHz throughout the
campaign. SDC rewritten with explicit ps units.

\end{record}

SRAM data paths.

\textbf{Finding}: Phase B1 expected −0.5 to −1.0 mW from downsizing the
timing-repair buffer fleet. The actual delta is noise. After Phase A
(Run 42) corrected CTS clustering, the 84 setup buffers Run 43 carries
are all necessary repairs on paths with \textless75 ps slack ---
\textbf{none were speculative repairs in the 75-100 ps slack window}.
Margin reduction is only PPA-meaningful when the resizer was repairing
paths inside the cut range; on this design, after Phase A, the
path-slack distribution clusters tightly behind the +5.969 ps WNS (per
the campaign\textquotesingle s known "co-critical path exposure" lesson)
with virtually no paths in 75--100 ps slack.

\textbf{Implication}: Power floor for this RTL/PDK with this floorplan
is locked at \textasciitilde27.27 mW. Further power gains require RTL
changes (more ICG clock gating, datapath sharing) or PDK changes (mixed
SVT/RVT --- requires library availability check). Run 43 remains the
signoff; Run 44 documented as a clean negative result for
future-campaign reference. \textbf{Config was reverted to Run 43
baseline (100/\allowbreak{}150/\allowbreak{}100/\allowbreak{}100) after
the probe.}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\end{record}

\subsection{Root-Cause Taxonomy}\label{cr:run:asap7:root-cause-taxonomy}

\begin{record}

{\footnotesize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*347/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*154/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*478/1000\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Category
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Runs Affected
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Lesson
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\textbf{Async-reset FF overhead} & 7--10 vs 11+ & \code{DFFASRHQNx1} QN
output ≈ +60--120 ps vs sync \code{DFFHQNx1}. Convert all pipeline FFs
to sync reset before timing closure. \\
\textbf{SDC \code{set_max_fanout} applied to CTS} & 12 & Never put
\code{set_max_fanout} in SDC if CTS leaf buffers exceed the limit. Move
to synthesis config. \\
\textbf{Die shrink → congestion} & 12, 14 & Shrinking die raises cell
utilization. Std-cell util \textgreater50\% increases routing
congestion. Keep to 35--45\% for this design. \\
\textbf{Co-critical path exposure} & 14, 18, 20, 38, 39 & Fixing the
worst path always exposes the next. Multiple cones share ±20 ps of each
other in this design. No single RTL fix closes timing alone. \\
\textbf{Config resizer no-op on depth-limited paths} & 38 & RSZ-0062
gives up when path is logic-depth-limited (21+ gate levels). Upsizing
drivers has zero effect on gate depth. \\
\textbf{ICG CTS imbalance} & 36 & ICG CLK inputs attract CTS sinks →
insertion-delay skew. Fix with \code{create_generated_clock} on GCLK
outputs +
\texttt{set\_\allowbreak{}false\_\allowbreak{}path\ -\allowbreak{}hold}. \\
\textbf{SDC units bug} & 7--31 & Liberty
\texttt{time\_\allowbreak{}unit:\allowbreak{}\ 1ps} +
\texttt{create\_\allowbreak{}clock\ -\allowbreak{}period\ 1.\allowbreak{}9}
(intended ns) → 1.9 ps effective period. Over-constrained STA by
\textasciitilde1000×. Always check time units before reading WNS. \\
\textbf{Power: SRAM dominates} & 35+ & SRAM macros = 48--73\% of power.
Only RTL-level clock gating (ICG) provides meaningful reduction. Die
shrink and density changes have \textless5\% power effect. \\
\textbf{Resizer margin cut after CTS fix = no-op} & 44 & Lowering
\code{PL_RESIZER_SETUP_SLACK_MARGIN} only helps when paths exist in the
cut slack range. Once CTS is balanced (Phase A) the repair fleet sits in
the \textless50 ps slack range; cutting margin from 100→75 ps removes no
repairs. Validate path-slack histogram before margin tuning. \\
\end{longtable}
}

}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\end{record}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{Addendum 2026-09-19: bead \code{ma7} sv2v
re-harden attempts (config.json, USE\_\allowbreak{}SYNLIG:false) ---
BLOCKED, not signed
off}{Addendum 2026-09-19: bead  sv2v re-harden attempts (config.json, USE\_SYNLIG:false) --- BLOCKED, not signed off}}\label{cr:run:asap7:addendum-2026-09-19-bead-ma7-sv2v-re-harden-attempts-configjson-use-synligfalse-blocked-not-signed-off}

\begin{record}

Out of scope for the Run 1--44 series above (that campaign is unaffected
and its own numbers stand as recorded), but recorded here since it
exercises the same \src{pnr/asap7/cpu/config.json} family. Bead
\code{u99}/\code{ma7} proved the \code{USE_SYNLIG:true} frontend used
through Run 44 miscompiles this macro\textquotesingle s regfile read
ports (see \src{CLAUDE.md}\textquotesingle s \bad{} block); the approved
remedy is \code{USE_SYNLIG:false}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  sv2v. Three re-harden attempts this session, none completed clean:
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \code{RUN_2026-09-19_14-59-15} --- failed at
  \code{Checker.LintErrors}: sv2v strips the inline
  \texttt{/\allowbreak{}*\ verilator\ lint\_\allowbreak{}off/\allowbreak{}on\ BLKLOOPINIT\ */\allowbreak{}}
  pragma \code{rv32i_icache.sv}/\code{rv32i_dcache.sv} rely on for their
  \texttt{for(j)\ valid\_\allowbreak{}array[j]\ <=\allowbreak{}\ .\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}}
  reset loops. Fixed: \code{asap7-cpu-sv2v} Makefile target now
  re-inserts an equivalent pragma into the generated file (verified via
  a standalone
  \texttt{verilator\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}lint-\allowbreak{}only}
  run matching LibreLane\textquotesingle s exact invocation args).
\item
  (no run directory kept) --- passed lint/\allowbreak{}synth, then
  \code{OpenROAD.ResizerTimingPostCTS} oscillated at
  \textasciitilde-142..-146 ps WNS / 3240 endpoints for 1650+
  \code{Iter} over 35+ min with no convergence --- the documented
  LibreLane 2.4.13 unbounded-\code{repair_timing} defect (bead
  \bead{bpp}, see \src{memory/pd/knowledge.md}). Fixed: added
  \code{PL_RESIZER_TIMING_MAX_PASSES=2} (the project\textquotesingle s
  own validated opt-in bound) to
  \code{config.json}/\code{config_3014.json}.
\item
  \code{RUN_2026-09-19_15-42-11} (kept for investigation, not pruned)
  --- got through synth, floorplan, GPL, repair, detailed placement,
  CTS, GRT and the now-bounded resizer cleanly, reaching
  \code{OpenROAD.DetailedRouting}. DRT then produced \textbf{108716 DRC
  violations} at 70\% completion --- \textasciitilde53x this
  block\textquotesingle s own best-ever prior routed baseline (2045
  violations, \code{RUN_2026-09-09_14-56-03}, bead \bead{e69}) ---
  concentrated as \code{DRT-0255} "Maze Route cannot find path" failures
  on SRAM macro pins
  (\code{u_core.u_dcache.gen_data_sram[N].u_data_sram/clk0},
  \code{/addr0}, \code{/data_gclk}; same failure class as bead
  \bead{ocm}, much larger magnitude). LibreLane\textquotesingle s
  catch-and-continue DRT patch let the flow limp forward, but
  \code{OpenROAD.RCX} then extracted \textbf{zero} parasitics ("Nothing
  is extracted out of 35390 nets") and \code{OpenROAD.STAPostPNR}
  hard-failed on the resulting empty SPEF.
\end{enumerate}

Ruled out: wrong OpenROAD binary (26Q2 pin-access fix confirmed active
via \code{check-asap7-openroad}); simple netlist-size growth
(\code{design__instance__count__stdcell} = 37575, only
\textasciitilde+9\% vs. the historical \textasciitilde75194-instance
total, not the \textasciitilde+25\% \code{valu_hls} saw). Root cause NOT
pinned down --- see bead \code{claude_verilog_test-lxv} for the full
diagnostic trail (including a utilization-based lead that was raised and
then substantially caveated: overall
\code{design__instance__utilization} is 54.8\% but the stdcell-only
figure is 40.2\%, inside this project\textquotesingle s own documented
35--45\% safe range, so a global-density explanation looks unlikely; the
localized macro-pin failure signature still points at bead
\bead{ocm}\textquotesingle s class instead).

\textbf{No macro views were regenerated.}
\code{pnr/asap7/soc/macro/rv32i_cpu_top.*} remain the old, Synlig-built,
\code{u99}/\code{ma7}-proven-corrupt views --- per the servicing
instructions, only a clean completed run may overwrite them, and none of
these three did. \code{ma7} stays OPEN. GPU macro re-harden remains
fully deferred (host-gated), unchanged from \code{ma7}\textquotesingle s
prior notes; GPU configs were switched to \code{USE_SYNLIG:false} for
consistency only, with no run attempted.

\end{record}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{Two single-variable experiments, both

\begin{record}

Both attempts changed exactly one config key against the failed
\code{RUN_2026-09-19_15-42-11} baseline, so each outcome is
interpretable on its own:

Experiment 2\textquotesingle s routing stalled the same way
(\texttt{Completing\ 50\%\ with\ 68951} →
\texttt{60\%/\allowbreak{}70\%\ with\ 126999}), then \code{STAPostPNR}
failed identically: RCX extracted nothing → empty SPEF →
\code{corner.tcl} non-zero exit.

\textbf{Why the flow was not simply truncated to force a "pass":} this
block\textquotesingle s own last real routed run
(\code{RUN_2026-09-09_14-56-03}, bead \bead{e69}) ran \code{STAPostPNR}
to completion on a real 61 MB extracted SPEF even with 2 045 DRC
violations outstanding. Disabling \code{RUN_SPEF_EXTRACTION} /
\code{RUN_MCSTA} here would not have matched that reference methodology
--- it would have reintroduced exactly the zero-wire-STA defect
(\code{xy6}/\code{8f3}) this project spent significant effort escaping.
A truncated run was therefore rejected as a false "clean".

\end{record}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\hold{} DECISION 2026-09-20: shelved
until a ≥32 GB host (beads \code{lxv}/\code{ma7} →
\code{2kn})}{ DECISION 2026-09-20: shelved until a ≥32 GB host (beads / → )}}\label{cr:run:asap7:pause-button-decision-2026-09-20-shelved-until-a-32-gb-host-beads-lxvma7-2kn}

\begin{record}

After five single-variable experiments refuted every config-level
hypothesis, the user chose to \textbf{defer} rather than authorise a
hard-macro LEF/\allowbreak{}pin-layout edit or re-baseline against the
pre-existing 2 045-violation routing baseline. \code{lxv} and \code{ma7}
are now formally blocked on \code{2kn} (the ≥32 GB host gate).

\textbf{Carry-forward risk:} the ASAP7 CPU and GPU macro views remain
the Synlig-built netlists that \code{u99}/\code{ma7} proved
\textbf{mis-execute a branch}, so every ASAP7
CPU/\allowbreak{}GPU/\allowbreak{}SoC PPA figure rests on a netlist with
a proven functional miscompile. The correctness fix \emph{is} applied in
the configs (\code{USE_SYNLIG:false} + sv2v); only a completed PD run to
regenerate the views is missing.

\textbf{Do not} resume with further config knob sweeps on this host ---
the exclusion list is exhaustive for config variables. On obtaining the
host, re-run the sv2v CPU re-harden \textbf{with the real power grid
first}: a PDN-bearing route has never been attempted on this block and
may change the detailed-routing picture on its own, before any
macro-geometry work is considered.

\textbf{Next-session candidates (none attempted), in order:} (1) diff
the Synlig-built vs sv2v-built netlists around
\code{u_core.u_dcache.gen_data_sram[*].u_data_sram} --- the failing pins
are always that SRAM\textquotesingle s
\code{clk0}/\code{csb0}/\code{addr0}/\code{din0}, so a structural
difference in naming, connectivity or an extra buffer layer at that
boundary is the strongest lead; (2) compare stdcell placement density in
the SRAM pin-access corridors between the old good run and this one; (3)
re-run at a relaxed target period (currently 780 ps) to test whether the
regression is resizer-driven cell bloat in the SRAM
fanin/\allowbreak{}fanout cones; (4) only then consider giving the CPU
macro its own pin-access treatment. Full trail on bead \bead{lxv}.

\end{record}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{Candidates 1+2 executed, read-only, no PD
run --- NARROWED, not closed (2026-09-19, bead
\code{lxv})}{Candidates 1+2 executed, read-only, no PD run --- NARROWED, not closed (2026-09-19, bead )}}\label{cr:run:asap7:candidates-12-executed-read-only-no-pd-run-narrowed-not-closed-2026-09-19-bead-lxv}

\begin{record}

No \code{e69} (\code{RUN_2026-09-09_14-56-03}, 2045-violation baseline)
artifacts survive on disk or \code{/nobackup}.
\code{RUN_2026-09-15_21-27-12} (\code{USE_SYNLIG:true}, same config
family, truncated at CTS --- no completed route) was used instead as the
best available Synlig-side comparator against the failing
\code{RUN_2026-09-19_15-42-11}, at matching pipeline stages
(\code{05-yosys-synthesis}, post-CTS DEF). Diagnostic script:
\src{tools/verif/gls/diff_asap7_sram_boundary.py}.

\textbf{Candidate 1 (netlist structural difference at the SRAM boundary)
--- REFUTED.} Compared post-synthesis gate netlists for
\code{u_core.u_dcache.gen_data_sram[0].u_data_sram}\textquotesingle s
\code{clk0}/\code{csb0}/ \code{din0[0]} drivers: \code{clk0} is driven
directly by \code{ICGx1_ASAP7_75t_R.GCLK} in both (no intermediate
buffer); \code{csb0} by an identical \code{NAND3xp33_ASAP7_75t_R} →
\code{INVxp67_ASAP7_75t_R} two-gate chain in both; \code{din0[0][0]} by
an identical \code{OAI32xp33_ASAP7_75t_R} with the same 5-input fanin
arity in both. Only auto-generated net numbers differ, exactly as
expected between two independent Yosys runs of structurally-identical
logic. 10 SRAM macro instances in both; instance count 36521 (good,
truncated at CTS) vs. 36775 (bad, CTS) is +0.7\%, not the
\textasciitilde9\% figure quoted pre-session. The \code{u99}/\code{ma7}
\code{OPT_MUXTREE} corruption (runtime-indexed regfile/\code{div_depth}
muxes) and this routing regression are almost certainly unrelated ---
the corruption is narrow (indexed-array muxes only) and does not touch
the SRAM control/\allowbreak{}data cones, which use static bit-sliced
combinational logic.

\textbf{Candidate 2 (local placement density in the SRAM pin-access
corridor) --- REFUTED.} The \code{PL_MACRO_HALO=2} keepout zone has 0
stdcells across the entire D-cache macro row band (x:0--60, y:47--99) in
both builds at post-CTS DEF (byte-identical: 0 vs. 0). The per-macro ICG
clock-gate cells that directly drive \code{clk0} (stable hierarchical
names) sit at an average Manhattan distance of 29.37 µm from their
macro\textquotesingle s \code{clk0} pin in the good build (sum 234.9 µm
over 8 data-SRAMs) vs. 29.11 µm in the bad build (sum 232.9 µm) ---
under 1\% delta, no systematic bias. Standard-cell placement immediately
around the SRAM macros is essentially identical between builds.

\textbf{New data point (not one of the four original candidates): GRT
itself is clean on the failing run.}
\code{37-openroad-globalrouting/openroad-globalrouting.log} on
\code{RUN_2026-09-19_15-42-11} reports 11.86\% total usage, 30.76\% max
per-layer (M3), and explicit
\texttt{0\ /\allowbreak{}\ 0\ /\allowbreak{}\ 0} (Max H / Max V / Total
Overflow) on every layer M1--M7, including the SRAM row region. The same
run\textquotesingle s DRT then produces 108716 violations. This rules
out a general capacity/\allowbreak{}demand explanation and confirms the
failure is detailed-routing-only, localized to the macro pin boundary.
Inspecting actual \code{DRT-0255} lines shows failures are not confined
to \code{clk0}/\code{csb0}/\code{addr0}/\code{din0} as originally scoped
--- \code{dout0} pins fail too (e.g.
\code{u_core.u_icache.gen_data_sram[1].u_data_sram/dout0[23]}), all
inside extremely narrow (2.73 µm × 2.73 µm) \code{routeBox} tiles
straddling the macro\textquotesingle s pin-bearing left edge. LEF
inspection confirms every signal pin on \code{sram_1rw_256x32_asap7.lef}
(\textasciitilde74 total:
\code{dout0}/\code{din0}/\code{addr0}/\code{csb0}/ \code{clk0}) is
\texttt{SHAPE\ ABUTMENT} at local \code{x=[0.000,0.024]} --- all crammed
onto one macro edge, a geometry meant for direct macro-to-macro abutment
rather than side-channel detailed-routing access through the fixed 2 µm
\code{macro_placement.cfg} gap. This access difficulty is pre-existing
and geometry-identical in both builds (same LEF, same
\code{macro_placement.cfg}), consistent with
\code{e69}\textquotesingle s own non-zero 2045-violation baseline on the
identical geometry --- the sv2v build\textquotesingle s
\textasciitilde53× worse count is a matter of degree in an
already-marginal detailed-routing situation, not a newly-introduced
structural or placement defect.

\textbf{Verdict: narrowed, not closed.} Cumulative exclusions: wrong
OpenROAD binary; netlist-size growth; global density;
\code{PL_MACRO_HALO} (exp 1); GRT M1 capacity (exp 2); structural
netlist difference at the failing pins (this session);
placement-clearance/\allowbreak{}distance difference around the macros
(this session); a genuine global routing
capacity/\allowbreak{}congestion problem (this session --- GRT is clean
on the failing run). Remaining, unexcluded explanation for the
\emph{magnitude} jump (2045 → 108716): DRT\textquotesingle s maze router
in these inherently marginal 2.73 µm pin-access tiles is sensitive to
fine-grained differences invisible to structural/\allowbreak{}averaged
comparison --- per-net track/\allowbreak{}access-point assignment order
(can legitimately differ between two independent synth
runs\textquotesingle{} net-name hashing with identical logic), and/or
\code{PL_RESIZER_TIMING_MAX_PASSES=2} (added only to this sv2v run to
dodge the unrelated resizer-oscillation defect, bead \bead{bpp}; absent
from the historical \code{e69}/\code{USE_SYNLIG:true} baseline) leaving
a different residual buffer distribution that marginally tips local
track demand over the edge in these tiles.

\textbf{Next step (not attempted --- costs a bounded run):} candidate 3,
relaxed \code{CLOCK_PERIOD} (e.g. 900--1000 ps) with the resizer pass
cap raised/\allowbreak{}removed, to test whether reducing resizer-driven
cell pressure reduces the \code{DRT-0255} count. A substantial
(order-of-magnitude) drop would confirm the resizer-cap hypothesis;
violations staying \textasciitilde100k+ regardless of period would
implicate the SRAM LEF/\allowbreak{}geometry itself (candidate 4,
macro-specific pin-access treatment) as the real fix, independent of the
frontend swap.

\end{record}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{Candidate 3 executed as two bounded PD
runs --- both REFUTED (2026-09-19, bead
\code{lxv})}{Candidate 3 executed as two bounded PD runs --- both REFUTED (2026-09-19, bead )}}\label{cr:run:asap7:candidate-3-executed-as-two-bounded-pd-runs-both-refuted-2026-09-19-bead-lxv}

\begin{record}

Two single-variable runs, each under
\texttt{systemd-\allowbreak{}run\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}scope\ -\allowbreak{}p\ MemoryMax=\allowbreak{}11500M\ -\allowbreak{}p\ MemorySwapMax=\allowbreak{}0},
foreground, logs/\allowbreak{}scratch on \code{/nobackup}. Ordering
rationale: the relaxed-period experiment was run first because raising
\code{PL_RESIZER_TIMING_MAX_PASSES} risks reproducing the exact
unbounded \code{repair_timing} oscillation (bead \bead{bpp}: 35+
min/pass, no convergence) that made the cap necessary in the first place
--- a real risk of exhausting the session on one non-terminating
experiment. Relaxing the period instead reduces resizer pressure, so it
was expected to be both safer and a direct test of the same hypothesis.

\textbf{Experiment A --- \code{CLOCK_PERIOD} 780 → 1000 ps}
(\code{config.json} \code{CLOCK_PERIOD} and
\code{constraints/asap7.sdc}\textquotesingle s
\texttt{create\_\allowbreak{}clock\ -\allowbreak{}period}, single
variable, all else identical to \code{RUN_2026-09-19_15-42-11}).
Confirms if DRC drops materially below 108716; refutes if unchanged
despite reduced resizer activity. Run \code{RUN_2026-09-19_19-48-00},
\textasciitilde36 min wall clock, completed through DRT/RCX.
\textbf{Result: 109,687 DRC violations} (+0.9\% vs. the 108716 baseline)
--- same
\code{RCX-0107}/\code{DRT-0418}/\code{DRT-0419}/\code{STAPostPNR}
failure signature. Cell-count check (post-DRT "Cell type report"):
Timing Repair Buffer 7189 → 6486 (−9.8\%), total instances 37585 → 36900
(−1.8\%) --- resizer activity dropped exactly as expected from looser
timing, but the DRC count did not follow. \textbf{REFUTED.}

\textbf{Experiment B --- \code{PL_RESIZER_TIMING_MAX_PASSES} 2 → 4}
(\code{config.json} only; \code{CLOCK_PERIOD}/ \code{asap7.sdc} restored
to the committed 780 ps baseline first, confirmed via an empty
\texttt{git\ diff} before launch). Tests the delta actually introduced
by this re-harden effort (the pass cap did not exist in the historical
\code{e69} baseline). Used a modest +2 rather than unbounded, to bound
the oscillation risk. Run \code{RUN_2026-09-19_20-26-19},
\textasciitilde48 min wall clock, \textbf{no
oscillation/\allowbreak{}hang observed} at this bound. \textbf{Result:
109,562 DRC violations} (+0.8\% vs. baseline) --- same failure
signature. Cell-count check: Timing Repair Buffer 7189 → 7341 (+2.1\%),
total instances 37585 → 37737 (+0.4\%) --- more resizer passes inserted
slightly more buffers, again with no material effect on the DRC count.
\textbf{REFUTED.} Config reverted to
\texttt{PL\_\allowbreak{}RESIZER\_\allowbreak{}TIMING\_\allowbreak{}MAX\_\allowbreak{}PASSES:\allowbreak{}\ 2}
post-experiment; \texttt{git\ diff} on both files confirmed empty before
commit.

\textbf{Cumulative verdict: both candidate-3 sub-experiments refuted.}
DRC violation count is invariant to resizer-driven cell-count changes in
either direction (−9.8\% buffers → +0.9\% DRC; +2.1\% buffers → +0.8\%
DRC) --- both land within \textasciitilde1\% of the 108716 baseline and
\textasciitilde53× the historical 2045-violation reference. This cleanly
excludes resizer pressure/\allowbreak{}cell bloat as the causal driver,
on top of the prior session\textquotesingle s exclusions (netlist
structural difference, placement clearance, global routing congestion).
The CPU sv2v re-harden \textbf{remains blocked}. The only unexcluded
explanation is the SRAM hard-macro\textquotesingle s own pin-access
geometry (\code{sram_1rw_256x32_asap7.lef}: all \textasciitilde74 signal
pins \texttt{SHAPE\ ABUTMENT} on a single edge) --- pre-existing and
marginal in both frontends (consistent with \code{e69}\textquotesingle s
own non-zero 2045-violation baseline on identical geometry), with the
sv2v build\textquotesingle s larger netlist tipping it over a cliff-edge
that timing/\allowbreak{}resizer tuning cannot walk back. \textbf{A fix
at this point most plausibly requires a hard-macro
LEF/\allowbreak{}pin-layout change (redistributing pins across multiple
edges, or widening the macro\textquotesingle s routing-access apron) ---
this needs explicit human approval before any macro view is touched, per
this project\textquotesingle s standing convention.} No macro view or
LEF was modified this session.

\end{record}

\subsubsection{Run 7 --- Baseline (Apr 26, 2026)}\label{cr:run:asap7-cpu:run-7-baseline-apr-26-2026}

\begin{record}

\textbf{WNS: −1147 ps \textbar{} fmax est. \textasciitilde466 MHz}

First complete ASAP7 LibreLane flow run. Establishes baseline PPA after
all infrastructure patches (RSZ-0089 set\_\allowbreak{}rc.tcl, DRT-0074
catch block, PDN non-fatal exit). 1.0 ns SDC target (bug: resolved to
1.0 ps).

\textbf{Infrastructure patches applied (all runs inherit):}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \code{set_rc.tcl} ASAP7 RC injection (RSZ-0089)
\item
  \code{drt.tcl} catch block for DRT-0074 non-fatal
\item
  \code{pdn.tcl} non-fatal exit (PDN-0179)
\item
  IO pin thickness ×8 (DRT-0074 valid WIDTHTABLE width)
\item
  \texttt{FP\_\allowbreak{}PDN\_\allowbreak{}RAIL\_\allowbreak{}WIDTH:\allowbreak{}\ 0.\allowbreak{}054}
  (PDN-0179)
\end{itemize}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{Run 11 (May 8, 2026)}\label{cr:run:asap7-cpu:run-11-may-8-2026}

\begin{record}

\textbf{WNS: −844.7 ps → +214 ps vs Run 9 \textbar{} Power: 31.81 mW
\textbar{} fmax est. \textasciitilde542 MHz}

\textbf{Critical discovery}: All 4,102+ setup violations trace to a
single FF \code{_54587_} --- the I-cache FSM state register, a
\code{DFFASRHQNx1} async-reset cell. The QN (inverted Q) output adds
extra inversion delay.

\textbf{Root cause}: D-cache tag comparison FF \code{_55931_} (821
fanout) and \code{_56972_} (785 fanout) were co-critical in Run 13 but
masked. EX2 broke the ALU path and exposed these.\\
\textbf{Lesson}: Multiple co-critical paths exist; eliminating one
reveals the next.

\subsubsection{Run 32 --- SDC Units Bug Fixed (May 18--19, 2026)}\label{cr:run:asap7-cpu:run-32-sdc-units-bug-fixed-may-1819-2026}

\begin{record}

\textbf{Milestone}: Discovered that
\texttt{create\_\allowbreak{}clock\ -\allowbreak{}period\ 1.\allowbreak{}9}
resolved to \textbf{1.9 ps} against ASAP7 Liberty\textquotesingle s
\texttt{time\_\allowbreak{}unit:\allowbreak{}\ 1ps}. All prior WNS
values represented path delays relative to \textasciitilde2 ps required
time. True design fmax was \textasciitilde1147 MHz throughout the
campaign. SDC rewritten with explicit ps units.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\end{record}

sinks → unbalanced insertion delay. SRAM clock latency 288 ps vs FF
latency 143 ps → CTS skew 62→\textbf{159 ps}. 4 hold violations on short
SRAM data paths.

Insight: relaxing CTS leaf clustering eliminated over-fragmentation; the
resizer no longer needed to compensate with hundreds of setup buffers.
Skew didn\textquotesingle t move --- structural CTS imbalance
(ICG-driven) is independent of leaf clustering.

caused the CTS engine to balance more aggressively → worst setup skew
dropped 51→29 ps (−45\%) as a second-order benefit. This is the new

\textasciitilde50\% lower skew vs Run 41}.

reduction would leave only \textasciitilde+1 ps margin (insufficient for
run-to-run CTS variation).

\textbf{Finding}: Phase B1 expected −0.5 to −1.0 mW from downsizing the
timing-repair buffer fleet. The actual delta is noise. After Phase A
(Run 42) corrected CTS clustering, the 84 setup buffers Run 43 carries
are all necessary repairs on paths with \textless75 ps slack ---
\textbf{none were speculative repairs in the 75-100 ps slack window}.
Margin reduction is only PPA-meaningful when the resizer was repairing
paths inside the cut range; on this design, after Phase A, the
path-slack distribution clusters tightly behind the +5.969 ps WNS (per
the campaign\textquotesingle s known "co-critical path exposure" lesson)
with virtually no paths in 75--100 ps slack.

\textbf{Implication}: Power floor for this RTL/PDK with this floorplan
is locked at \textasciitilde27.27 mW. Further power gains require RTL
changes (more ICG clock gating, datapath sharing) or PDK changes (mixed
SVT/RVT --- requires library availability check). Run 43 remains the
signoff; Run 44 documented as a clean negative result for
future-campaign reference. \textbf{Config was reverted to Run 43
baseline (100/\allowbreak{}150/\allowbreak{}100/\allowbreak{}100) after
the probe.}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\end{record}

\subsection{Root-Cause Taxonomy}\label{cr:run:asap7-cpu:root-cause-taxonomy}

\begin{record}

{\footnotesize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*347/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*154/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*478/1000\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Category
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Runs Affected
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Lesson
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\textbf{Async-reset FF overhead} & 7--10 vs 11+ & \code{DFFASRHQNx1} QN
output ≈ +60--120 ps vs sync \code{DFFHQNx1}. Convert all pipeline FFs
to sync reset before timing closure. \\
\textbf{SDC \code{set_max_fanout} applied to CTS} & 12 & Never put
\code{set_max_fanout} in SDC if CTS leaf buffers exceed the limit. Move
to synthesis config. \\
\textbf{Die shrink → congestion} & 12, 14 & Shrinking die raises cell
utilization. Std-cell util \textgreater50\% increases routing
congestion. Keep to 35--45\% for this design. \\
\textbf{Co-critical path exposure} & 14, 18, 20, 38, 39 & Fixing the
worst path always exposes the next. Multiple cones share ±20 ps of each
other in this design. No single RTL fix closes timing alone. \\
\textbf{Config resizer no-op on depth-limited paths} & 38 & RSZ-0062
gives up when path is logic-depth-limited (21+ gate levels). Upsizing
drivers has zero effect on gate depth. \\
\textbf{ICG CTS imbalance} & 36 & ICG CLK inputs attract CTS sinks →
insertion-delay skew. Fix with \code{create_generated_clock} on GCLK
outputs +
\texttt{set\_\allowbreak{}false\_\allowbreak{}path\ -\allowbreak{}hold}. \\
\textbf{SDC units bug} & 7--31 & Liberty
\texttt{time\_\allowbreak{}unit:\allowbreak{}\ 1ps} +
\texttt{create\_\allowbreak{}clock\ -\allowbreak{}period\ 1.\allowbreak{}9}
(intended ns) → 1.9 ps effective period. Over-constrained STA by
\textasciitilde1000×. Always check time units before reading WNS. \\
\textbf{Power: SRAM dominates} & 35+ & SRAM macros = 48--73\% of power.
Only RTL-level clock gating (ICG) provides meaningful reduction. Die
shrink and density changes have \textless5\% power effect. \\
\textbf{Resizer margin cut after CTS fix = no-op} & 44 & Lowering
\code{PL_RESIZER_SETUP_SLACK_MARGIN} only helps when paths exist in the
cut slack range. Once CTS is balanced (Phase A) the repair fleet sits in
the \textless50 ps slack range; cutting margin from 100→75 ps removes no
repairs. Validate path-slack histogram before margin tuning. \\
\end{longtable}
}

}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\end{record}

\section{ASAP7 P\&R Run History --- GPU-Lite SIMT Compute Engine}\label{cr:run:asap7-gpu}

\begin{record}

\textbf{PDK}: ASAP7 7nm predictive FinFET \textbf{Tool}: LibreLane +
OpenROAD \textbf{Design}: \code{gpu_top} (single compute unit, 8 lanes,
8 warps; 16 KB shared memory via \code{sram_1rw_128x32} macros)
\textbf{Target}: Max fmax with 0 setup/\allowbreak{}hold violations, 0
internal-net DRC, 0 antenna \textbf{Branch}: \code{phase-4-pd-enhance}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Caveat 2026-10-10 (bead \bead{47lf}, \ghref{254}) --- every run
below, and the macro views exported from them, predate an RTL fix.}
\code{gpu_top} held its warp count in 3 bits and capped it at 7, so
\code{BLOCK_X} 57..64 ran 7 of 8 warps. Fixed in
\src{rtl/gpu/gpu_pkg.sv}, \code{gpu_top.sv}, \code{warp_scheduler.sv}
(\code{WARP_CNT_W} = 4): the scheduler\textquotesingle s
\code{n_warps_q} gains one flop (\code{n_warps_w} is combinational) and
the issue / \code{all_done} compares widen by one bit --- a handful of
cells, not expected to move the PPA below. \textbf{No PD run was made}:
the GPU cannot be re-hardened on this host (beads \code{ma7} /
\code{lxv} / \code{2kn}), so \code{pnr/asap7/soc/macro/gpu_top.*} are
functionally behind the RTL (in addition to the Synlig-miscompile caveat
of \code{ma7}). Re-harden when the host is available. \textbf{Caveat
2026-10-10 (bead \bead{q6w0}, \ghref{261}) --- the macro views are further
behind the RTL.} \code{GPU_CTRL.reset} now does a synchronous
quiesce-then-clear (\code{soft_clr}) across \code{gpu_top},
\code{warp_scheduler}, \code{gpu_compute_unit} and
\code{gpu_command_queue}: a handful of control flops (\code{rst_pend_q},
\code{halt_q}, \code{if_outst_q}) plus a synchronous clear term on the
scheduler/\allowbreak{}pipeline valid flops and \code{gpu_error_o}; no
new runtime-indexed mux, no change to the asynchronous reset. Small, not
expected to move the PPA above. \textbf{No PD run was made} (beads
\code{ma7} / \code{lxv} / \code{2kn}; same situation as the \code{47lf}
note above): \code{pnr/asap7/soc/macro/gpu_top.*} still behave as the
pre-fix design, in which \code{CTRL.reset} cannot leave ERROR. Re-harden
when the host is available.
\end{quote}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\end{record}

\subsubsection{Signoff caveats (documented, not regressions --- carried
by the prior 500 MHz run too)}\label{cr:run:asap7-gpu:signoff-caveats-documented-not-regressions-carried-by-the-prior-500-mhz-run-too}

\begin{record}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{PDN connectivity not closed.}
  \texttt{design\_\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}power\_\allowbreak{}grid\_\allowbreak{}violation\_\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}count\ =\allowbreak{}\ 9,\allowbreak{}557,\allowbreak{}658}
  (VDD 4.78 M / VSS 4.78 M); \code{PSM-0069} check-connectivity fails on
  VDD/VSS; \code{PDN-0179} unable to repair all channels.
  \textbf{Identical count to the 500 MHz run} --- a systematic
  block-level PDN gap, not introduced here. Defer the channel-repair /
  PDN fix to SoC-level power planning (Phase 5).
\item
  \textbf{325× \code{DRT-0074} "no access point"} --- all on top-level
  I/O ports (\code{clk}, \code{rst_n}, \code{s_axil_*}, \code{m_axi_*},
  \code{gpu_irq_o}). Zero internal-net DRC; \code{DRT-0073/0075} shorts
  / spacing = 0;
  \texttt{design\_\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}violations\ =\allowbreak{}\ 0}.
  Block-level I/O artifact that resolves when the SoC places real pins
  on routing tracks.
\item
  \textbf{Timing is post-GRT estimated.} Metrics come from step
  \code{39-openroad-stamidpnr-3} (post-global-route). \code{STAPostPNR}
  and \code{RCX} are gated off, so there is no SPEF-based sign-off STA.
  Numbers are optimistic but use the \textbf{same methodology as the 500
  MHz run}, so the run-to-run comparison is apples-to-apples. A
  confirmation re-run with \code{STAPostPNR} + \code{RCX} enabled is the
  recommended next step before locking the number.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{quote}
\warn{} \textbf{Bead \code{0p6} (2026-09-16): this is worse than
"optimistic" --- it is \code{OpenROAD.STAMidPNR-3} with the specific
zero-wire defect found by bead \code{claude_verilog_test-8f3}.} That
step\textquotesingle s \code{sta/corner.tcl} calls
\texttt{estimate\_\allowbreak{}parasitics\ -\allowbreak{}global\_\allowbreak{}routing}
on a loaded ODB \textbf{without an in-session \code{global_route}}, and
a fresh CPU-block measurement (same LibreLane 2.4.13 toolchain family)
showed 94.44\% of drivers unannotated under this exact failure mode,
with the resulting STA falsely reporting 0/0/0 setup and hold violations
everywhere --- matching this run\textquotesingle s own "0 vios" rows
above. The fix, \code{STA_POSTGRT_INSESSION_GRT=1}, is now applied to
the shared LibreLane tree, but \textbf{this GPU run\textquotesingle s
own artifacts are gone} (\code{pnr/asap7/gpu/runs/} and
\code{/nobackup/asap7_gpu_runs/} are both empty --- \code{/nobackup}
wipe, bead \bead{alq}, 2026-09-15), so it cannot be re-measured
directly; a fresh run is required to get an honest number.

\textbf{Not re-run as part of bead \bead{0p6}} --- explicitly deferred
pending authorization, per that bead\textquotesingle s own scope
constraint (another agent, bead \bead{0ah}, runs concurrent jobs up to
9.5 GB on this 15.9 GB host). Cost estimate, extrapolated from this
project\textquotesingle s own GPU-block history rather than assumed: the
GPU is the largest ASAP7 block in this repo (\textasciitilde485,842
stdcells vs the CPU block\textquotesingle s
\textasciitilde36,290-instance scale that bead
\bead{8f3}\textquotesingle s CPU-block validation cost
\textasciitilde295 MiB / +0.5 s extra for the in-session re-route
alone), it needed \textbf{13.5+ hours} for global routing to even
converge in an earlier campaign (\src{memory/pd/knowledge.md} §GPU
ASAP7, \code{RUN_2026-05-26_20-00-11}) before the
M8/\allowbreak{}M9-layer + \code{GRT_ADJUSTMENT} fix landed, and
detailed-routing/\allowbreak{}repair passes on this design have peaked
at \textbf{14+ GiB VmHWM} and were repeatedly killed by
\code{systemd-oomd}\textquotesingle s 50\%/20s memory-pressure policy
even after the true cause (pressure, not capacity) was root-caused. A
full fresh run to \code{OpenROAD.STAMidPNR-3} with
\code{STA_POSTGRT_INSESSION_GRT=1} --- which adds a second in-session
\code{global_route} invocation on top of the flow\textquotesingle s own
--- should be expected to cost \textbf{several hours wall-clock and
9--14+ GiB peak RSS}, i.e. it does not comfortably fit this
host\textquotesingle s stated concurrent-job budget and needs dedicated,
oomd-quiesced host time scheduled separately from bead
\bead{0ah}\textquotesingle s work. \textbf{Remains an open follow-up},
not closed by \code{0p6}.
\end{quote}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\end{record}

\subsubsection{Step-37 runtime note}\label{cr:run:asap7-gpu:step-37-runtime-note}

\begin{record}

\code{repair_design_postgrt} runs full GRT twice plus a
multi-thousand-iteration repair loop on the \textasciitilde485
K-instance design --- the dominant wall-clock cost. Levers explored:
\code{DRT_THREADS} 4→12, die-area growth, and (deferred) disabling
post-GRT resizer timing. See \src{memory/pd/knowledge.md} and
\cref{cr:status:phase-status} for the full step-37 analysis.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\end{record}

\subsection{Macro Views (Phase-5 hand-off)}\label{cr:run:asap7-gpu:macro-views-phase-5-hand-off}

\begin{record}

The signoff DB is exported as ASAP7 hard-macro views for SoC
integration:

\begin{lstlisting}[language=bash]
cd pnr && make macro-views-asap7 BLOCK=gpu          # latest GPU run
make macro-views-asap7 BLOCK=gpu RUN=asap7/gpu/runs/RUN_2026-05-28_06-29-48
\end{lstlisting}

Output → \src{pnr/asap7/gpu/macro/}:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \code{gpu_top.lef} --- abstract LEF (gitignored, regenerated on
  demand)
\item
  \code{gpu_top__nom_tt_025C_0p7V.lib} --- timing model (committed)
\item
  \code{gpu_top.nl.v.gz} --- flat post-route netlist, gzipped
  (\textasciitilde9.6 MB; raw \textasciitilde103 MB exceeds
  GitHub\textquotesingle s 100 MB limit). Run
  \texttt{gunzip\ -\allowbreak{}k\ gpu\_\allowbreak{}top.\allowbreak{}nl.\allowbreak{}v.\allowbreak{}gz}
  to restore for full-flat sim/LEC.
\end{itemize}

\end{record}
